commutes with the Euclidean translations, its induced map \(\widehat D\) on
the completed null quotients obeys

$$
\widehat D e^{-\ell K_W}
=
e^{-\ell K_L}\widehat D.
$$

Laplace-transform resolvent intertwining then gives spectral-projection
intertwining and

$$
\widehat D e^{-ictK_W}
=
e^{-ictK_L}\widehat D.
$$

Thus a noninjective realization can be exactly covariant for both the
Euclidean semigroups and the reconstructed Lorentzian clocks. But if a
surjection \(r\) satisfies \(rA=Br\), every polynomial identity of \(A\)
passes to \(B\). Hence

$$
A^2=A,\quad B\text{ unitary}
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\boxed{B=I}.
$$

A conditional-expectation projection cannot itself become a nontrivial
clock. Likewise a positive contraction is unitary only when it is the
identity; \(e^{-\ell K}\) can be unitary at positive \(\ell\) only for
\(K=0\). Finally, the projection
\(\mathcal H\oplus\mathcal N\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal H\) intertwines
\(e^{-\ell K}\oplus e^{-\ell R}\) with \(e^{-\ell K}\) for arbitrary
positive \(K,R\). One fixed nonfaithful quotient is therefore compatible
with arbitrary local gaps and selects none of them.

[[algebra/faithful-descent-rigidity-and-noiseless-unitarity|Faithful descent
gives an exact firewall.]] Let \(N\subseteq M\), let \(E:M\to N\) be a faithful
normal conditional expectation, let \((\Phi_t)_{t\geq0}\) be a
point-ultraweakly continuous normal UCP semigroup, and define the expected
maps

$$
\alpha_t:=E\Phi_t|_N
$$

Suppose that every \(\alpha_t\) is a \(*\)-automorphism. This is an algebraic
hypothesis on the expected maps, not merely unitarity of a corner or Hilbert
compression. The two Schwarz inequalities for
\(\Phi_t\) and \(E\), followed by faithfulness, force

$$
\Phi_t|_N=\alpha_t,
\qquad
N\subseteq\operatorname{MD}(\Phi_t).
$$

Thus faithful coarse-graining does not manufacture an exact local automorphism: it
reveals a sector that was already invariant and noiseless. A faithful normal
state satisfying \(\varphi\Phi_t=\varphi\) makes the local GNS carrier reduce
the global contraction. The additional condition \(\varphi E=\varphi\) gives
\(E\Phi_t=\alpha_tE\) and the modular compatibility used for the KMS
standard-form realization. If the corresponding left-GNS implementation is
self-adjoint, or the corresponding symmetric implementation is self-adjoint
in the KMS case, the descended continuous-parameter unitary satisfies
\(U_t=U_{t/2}^2=I\). This conclusion does not apply to one isolated
self-adjoint channel, for which a nontrivial involution is possible. A
nontrivial local clock instead requires a reversible skew-adjoint component
in the relevant \(L^2\) implementation or an independently soldered clock
parameter; an inner Hamiltonian in \(N\) is extra structure. A globally
nonautomorphic semigroup may still coexist with a local unitary group on a
reducing summand, but the loss occurs in the complement. Nonfaithful descent
can hide genuine leakage, which is why formation, attenuation, and clock
evolution must remain separately typed.

The same typing resolves what Hessian response can contribute. For a regular
readout \(F\) with differential \(J=dF\), the pulled-back response is

$$
G(v,w)=g_{\mathrm{BKM}}(Jv,Jw),
\qquad
G^\flat=J^\vee g_{\mathrm{BKM}}^\flat J.
$$

When the target BKM form is positive definite on the declared regular tangent
class, \(\operatorname{rad}G=\ker J\). For a family, the radical is the
intersection of the readout kernels. Hence a
finite family of finite-dimensional Hessian targets cannot have a positive
lower frame on an infinite-dimensional vacuum complement. A viable
whole-to-local construction needs a joint analysis map with a regulator-uniform
lower frame--possibly a finite family of infinite-dimensional targets, an
infinite or direct-integral family, or a same-carrier operator with its own
proved vacuum-complement edge. This is the exact mathematical content now available for
the intuition that each local fact implicates the whole: no physical direction
may remain invisible to every local comparison.

The local-unitary carrier has an additional exact obstruction. For a faithful
regional state and the declared algebraic class of localized self-adjoint
inner-unitary directions
\(u_A=(A-\omega_B(A)\mathbf1)\Omega\), the regional-state differential is

$$
\widetilde J_Bu_A(x)=i\omega_B([x,A]),
$$

so its algebraic kernel is precisely the centered
\(\omega_B\)-centralizer. Its closure or a further restriction/recovery map
can add null directions; on the full projective carrier, commutant-unitary
purification directions are invisible as well. If \(T_B\) is the regional BKM analysis map and \(D_{\mathrm{cat}}\) the
categorical defect on a common form core, then
\(q_B\geq b\,e_{\mathrm{cat}}\) holds exactly when
\(D_{\mathrm{cat}}^{1/2}\) factors boundedly through \(T_B\). Thus any
centralizer direction charged by the categorical defect kills the comparison.
In infinite dimension a vacuum-only joint kernel is merely necessary: the
regional family must have a regulator-uniform
\(D_{\mathrm{cat}}\)-relative lower frame. Reeh--Schlieder density supplies
neither that bound nor an energy-form core, and a restriction-loss form can
have further recovery nulls even after the centralizer test passes.

The Wilson and categorical tests then isolate the same-carrier route surviving
the carriers audited here. Multiplication by the restricted fundamental Wilson
character realizes the expected fusion rule, but at classical normalization
there is no normalizable invariant GNS vector--only a formal distributional
\(1\)-eigenstate or evaluation functional at the flat connection--and its
centered \(L^2\) edge is zero. Replacing
the classical dimension \(7\) by the quantum dimension \(\delta_q>7\) adds a
positive scalar translation, but that scalar cancels from normalized Gibbs
states and excitation-energy differences; the remainder only changes the
Wilson coupling. Canonical subfactor hypergroup actions fail oppositely: their
UCP maps fix the entire smaller algebra, so the common GNS fixed space is much
larger than the vacuum.

What survives is a precise conditional construction. One must build a family
of state-preserving UCP maps directly on the neutral observable algebra that
satisfies the normalized fusion law, prove that its GNS fusion representation
is annular or tube admissible, and prove

$$
\bigcap_\alpha\operatorname{Fix}(V_\alpha)=\mathbb C\Omega.
$$

Quantum-\(G_2\) property \((T)\) would then supply the dimensionless lower
edge for a specified symmetric Kazhdan averaging element. A claim for one
fundamental generator additionally requires its corresponding universal
estimate. On a real tangent core, an isometric BKM--GNS intertwiner and weighted
coverage can make the full regional relative-entropy half-Hessian dominate the
categorical defect, with coefficient \(b_D/4\) in the present normalization.
That does not imply domination by the smaller restriction-loss form; the
stronger wall reading needs that comparison independently. Either route still
requires a positive Hermitian extension to the complex energy-form core and
the localized entropy--energy upper bound. Only this final comparison converts
categorical rigidity into clock energy.
The construction is not complete, but the missing object is now typed closely
enough to build or refute: a vacuum-ergodic, annularly admissible normalized
fusion action by state-preserving UCP maps, with uniform physical coverage,
not a character multiplier, a
finite Hessian, or a freely normalized Markov step.

### Modular tomography and the process-category completion

The centralizer obstruction now has an exact completion. For a family of
faithful normal states \(\mathcal F=\{\varphi_i\}\) on one von Neumann
algebra, define

$$
d_{\varphi_i}A(x)=i\varphi_i([x,A]).
$$

Then

$$
\ker\bigoplus_i d_{\varphi_i}
=
\left(\bigcap_iM_{\varphi_i}\right)_{\rm sa}.
$$

Choosing \(\varphi_0\in\mathcal F\) and Connes cocycles
\(u_i(t)=[D\varphi_i:D\varphi_0]_t\) gives the sharper one-carrier formula

$$
\boxed{
\bigcap_iM_{\varphi_i}
=
M_{\varphi_0}\cap\{u_i(t):i,t\}'.}
$$

Moreover, the intersection of the centralizers of all faithful normal states
is exactly the center; a countable family suffices when the predual is
separable. [[library/ergodic-states-on-type-iii-1-factors-and-ergodic-actions/inq|Marrakchi and Vaes]] prove the striking one-state refinement: a
nontrivial separable-predual von Neumann algebra has a faithful normal state
with scalar centralizer exactly when it is a type-III$_1$ factor, and such
states are dense \(G_\delta\). This removes the *algebraic* objection that a
type-III regional carrier must have unavoidable local-unitary blind
directions. It does not show that the physical vacuum restriction is such a
state. Their modular representation is weakly mixing on the vacuum
complement, not spectrally gapped.

The Connes cocycle itself must not be retyped as a mass generator. In general

$$
u_i(t+s)=u_i(t)\sigma_t^{\varphi_0}(u_i(s)),
$$

so it is not an ordinary one-parameter group and Stone's theorem does not
apply. Its correct role here is tomographic: it computes how the blind
centralizer changes between state presentations.

A second exact result supplies a quantitative benchmark on the original
type-III carrier. Let a property-$(T)$ group preserve a faithful normal state
and act by automorphisms \(\alpha_g\). For a finite symmetric Kazhdan
probability \(\mu\), the UCP average

$$
\mathsf T_\mu=\sum_g\mu(g)\alpha_g
$$

has GNS defect

$$
\langle\xi,(I-V_\mu)\xi\rangle
=
\frac12\sum_g\mu(g)\|U_g\xi-\xi\|^2.
$$

For a Kazhdan pair \((S,\kappa)\), this gives

$$
I-V_\mu
\geq
\frac{\mu_{\min}\kappa^2}{2}(I-P_\Gamma).
$$

If the invariant GNS space is only the vacuum line, this is a genuine
dimensionless same-carrier edge. Poissonization produces a state-preserving
quantum Markov semigroup that is strictly contractive on the vacuum
complement and hence is not an automorphism there. At every finite parameter
it remains invertible as a bounded linear map, but its inverse is generally
not UCP or contractive; this is Markov distinction decay, not yet
noninjective forgetting or a factive event. [[library/the-kms-and-gns-spectral-gap-of-quantum-markov-semigroups/inq|Wirth's 2026
recent preprint]]
operator-monotone interpolation theorem then transfers its GNS decay rate to
every observable \(f\)-norm, including BKM, on an arbitrary von Neumann
algebra. It does not automatically transfer the edge to the inverse BKM
metric on state tangents, nor to physical clock energy.

This yields the cleanest current algebraic architecture. The ambient process
category contains normal UCP arrows; its maximal reversible core contains
the automorphisms represented unitarily by local QFT. But three arrows remain
distinct: a nonfaithful realization
\(\Pi:\mathcal C_{\rm whole}\to\mathcal C_{\rm local}\), a Markov
endoprocess \(\mathsf P_t:\mathcal A(O)\to\mathcal A(O)\), and a local clock
automorphism \(\alpha_s\). The Markov defect can represent the wall residue
only after a theorem constructs \(\Pi\) and relates its response to that
Dirichlet form. Chiral or oriented structure may live in the nonsymmetric
process data, while a symmetric average extracts the positive coercive form.
[[library/quantum-operations-on-conformal-nets/inq|Rigorous quantum
operations on conformal nets]] prove that compatible, vacuum-preserving,
covariant UCP families on type-III local algebras are possible and may form
hypergroups. They do not yet provide the four-dimensional Yang--Mills
operation.

### Trace-Dirichlet completion of the missing arrow

[[trace-dirichlet-descent/inq|Trace Dirichlet descent]] now separates two
opposite operations. If \(\pi:X\to Y\), \(\nu=\pi_*\mu\), and
\(Jf=f\circ\pi\), the whole form first defines the observable-pullback form

$$
\mathcal E^\leftarrow[f]=\mathcal E_W[Jf].
$$

The map \(J\) is an isometric *-homomorphism on bounded observables and
commutes with normal contractions. Consequently, when the whole form is
closed and Dirichlet and the restricted domain is dense, this form is closed
and Markovian; every whole Poincare inequality passes to it exactly. The state
moves from whole to local, while the local observable moves in the opposite
direction to be tested by the whole response.

[[trace-dirichlet-descent/standard-form-pullback-and-reducing-wall|The
standard-form theorem]] proves the noncommutative version. Let
\(\iota:N\hookrightarrow M\) be a normal unital faithful inclusion and
\(\varphi\) a faithful normal state. If \(N\) is invariant under the modular
group of \(\varphi\), Takesaki's theorem supplies a
\(\varphi\)-preserving expectation. The induced isometry
\(V:L^2(N,\varphi|_N)\to L^2(M,\varphi)\) preserves the real structure and
the Markov interval at every matrix amplification. Hence

$$
\mathcal E_N[\eta]=\mathcal E_M[V\eta]
$$

is closed and completely Dirichlet whenever \(\mathcal E_M\) is and the
restricted domain is dense. Modularity and conservativity require their
separate intertwining and zero-energy-vacuum hypotheses. This applies to
expected Type-III inclusions without introducing a density matrix.

The sharper least-cost question uses the adjoint conditional coordinate
\(q=J^*\). More generally, let

$$
q:D(\mathcal E_W)\longrightarrow\mathcal H_L
$$

be the whole-to-local carrier map, and define

$$
\boxed{
\check{\mathcal E}[\varphi]
=
\inf_{qw=\varphi}\mathcal E_W[w].}
$$

If the infimal pushforward is densely defined, closed, and Markovian, its
positive self-adjoint representative \(D_q\) generates a symmetric local
sub-Markov semigroup. Conservativity requires the distinguished order-unit
vector to lie in its kernel. In a standard form, complete Dirichletness and
conservativity are the additional hypotheses needed for a unital quantum
Markov semigroup. They do not follow from positivity of an arbitrary
noncommutative Schur complement.

The local edge of this infimal branch has an exact whole-register criterion:

$$
D_q\geq\kappa(I-P_0)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\mathcal E_W[w]
\geq
\kappa\|(I-P_0)qw\|^2
\quad
(w\in D(\mathcal E_W)).
$$

One implication uses
\(\check{\mathcal E}[qw]\leq\mathcal E_W[w]\); the converse takes the
infimum over every lift. This is stronger than a kernel calculation. A
globally gapless family is compatible with a gapped local trace exactly when
all low-response sequences become locally vacuum-like at the corresponding
uniform rate.

When every fibre has a linear harmonic minimizer \(h\), and \(q,h\) extend
to a bounded split retraction of the completed carriers, the split idempotent
\(e=hq\) yields

$$
\operatorname B(\mathcal H_L)
\cong
e\operatorname B(W)e.
$$

[[algebra/retract-corners-and-local-unitarity|The corner theorem]] therefore
places every local unitary \(U\) at \(hUq\). It is invertible relative to the
corner identity \(e\) and, for noninjective \(q\), noninvertible relative to
the whole identity \(I_W\). If \(U\) preserves the trace form, the whole
representative obeys

$$
\mathcal E_W[hUqw]
=
\check{\mathcal E}[qw]
\leq
\mathcal E_W[w],
$$

with equality on the harmonic image. The vertical residue is removed once;
the retained action is reversible. This is an exact algebraic realization of
asymmetry preceding local symmetry, but it still does not identify the
positive trace generator \(D_q\) with the self-adjoint clock generator of
\(U_t\).

The two local forms are ordered,

$$
\kappa\operatorname{Var}_{\nu}(f)
\leq
\check{\mathcal E}[f]
\leq
\mathcal E^\leftarrow[f],
$$

but they answer different questions. The pullback asks how a declared local
observable responds when tested in the whole; the infimum asks for the least
whole cost compatible with a conditional local presentation. The compressed
family \(J^*e^{-sD_W}J\) is a third construction and need not be a semigroup
without lumpability.

The standard-form theorem identifies the exact equality gate. If
\(p=VV^*\) reduces the whole form--equivalently, it commutes with the
self-adjoint \(L^2\) semigroup--then hidden and retained directions are
form-orthogonal, and observable pullback, invariant restriction, infimal
quotient, and semigroup compression all agree. Expectedness alone does not
imply reduction. This is why Type III repairs the carrier problem for the
safe pullback without magically repairing an arbitrary short.

Classical trace-Dirichlet theory proves the infimal construction for suitable
boundaries: [[library/generalizing-dirichlet-to-neumann-operators/inq|Li]]
identifies the Dirichlet-to-Neumann generator with the trace form and the
time-changed boundary process, while
[[library/the-dirichlet-to-neumann-operator-on-rough-domains/inq|Arendt and
ter Elst]] construct the boundary contraction semigroup by form methods on
rough domains. The new noncommutative obligation is precise: construct the
physical expected standard-form inclusion and, if the least-cost reading is
required without reduction, prove that its infimal pushforward remains closed
and completely Dirichlet. The Yang--Mills obligation is stronger:
prove the lift inequality uniformly on every neutral physical direction
without defining \(\mathcal E_W\) from the Hamiltonian whose gap is sought.

Two bounded specializations clarify what a second wall contributes.
[[contemporary-puzzles/yang-mills-mass-gap/two-expectation-angle-process|The
palindromic expectation process]] starts from tracial expectations with GNS
projections \(p,q\). The UCP map \(E_PE_QE_P\) has positive implementation
\(pqp\), so Poissonization yields the completely Dirichlet generator

$$
L_{P,Q}=I-pqp
\geq
(1-c_F^2)(I-r),
\qquad
r=p\wedge q.
$$

The whole common range \(r\mathcal H\), not merely one vacuum vector, remains
fixed. The process therefore becomes a physical gap carrier only after a
uniformly bounded-below map from the physical vacuum complement into
\((I-r)\mathcal H\). A fixed-index spin-model family has
\(1-c_F^2=\sin^2\delta\to0\), proving that inclusion index and one Q-system do
not supply the required uniform relative position.

The variational counterpart starts with the positive two-wall form rather
than a channel. For \(C=PQP|_{P\mathcal H}\),
[[contemporary-puzzles/yang-mills-mass-gap/shorted-response-filtration-and-the-leak-cocycle#Paired walls produce an exact tangential response|paired-wall shorting]]
gives

$$
S_{P\mathcal H}\!\left(\alpha(I-P)+\beta(I-Q)\right)
=
\alpha\beta(I-C)(\alpha I+\beta C)^{-1}.
$$

The reduced floor is
\(\alpha\beta(1-c_F^2)/(\alpha+\beta c_F^2)\), whereas shorting a single
wall \(I-P\) to its own retained carrier gives zero. In the stationary
past--future OS member, the equal-weight short is
\(\tanh(daH_T/\hbar c)\). That identity is useful because it types the
bounded wall response as a nonlinear presentation of clock energy; it is not
an independent gap proof when the history projections were built from the
same transfer process.

[[contemporary-puzzles/yang-mills-mass-gap/gauge-dirichlet-trace-carrier|The
Wilson descent test]] reaches a genuine regulated gauge carrier without using
the transfer spectrum. In the Shen--Zhu--Zhu normalization, the
strong-coupling Langevin form obeys a volume-uniform Poincare inequality with
\(K_S=N/2-8N|\beta|(d-1)>0\) for \(SU(N)\). For the interface projection,
the multiplicative cylinder pullback gives a closed Dirichlet form satisfying

$$
\mathcal E^{\mathrm{cyl}}_\Sigma[f]
\geq
K_S\|f-\nu_\Sigma(f)\mathbf1\|_2^2.
$$

Conditional expectation \(q_\Sigma\) onto the same interface contracts
centered \(L^2\), so its infimal trace also satisfies exactly

$$
\check{\mathcal E}_\Sigma[f]
\geq
K_S\|f-\nu_\Sigma(f)\mathbf1\|_2^2.
$$

This is the first present example in which a nontrivial Yang--Mills whole
form forces a positive interface response without reading the transfer
spectrum. The pullback branch is already Markov; the infimal branch adds a
nonfaithful boundary map and forces the abstract lift inequality.
Reflection-Markov factorization and complete interface coverage would
transport either positive operator isometrically to the OS Hilbert carrier.
That Hilbert conjugation does not by itself identify a quantum Markov
semigroup on the represented observable algebra or the physical Hamiltonian.
The remaining gaps are not cosmetic. A six-vertex classical graph gives an exact
conditional-expectation trace with
\(\check{\mathcal E}(|f|)>\check{\mathcal E}(f)\), so even classical
Markovianity does not follow from the conditional infimum. The safe cylinder
form must still cover the complete neutral observable algebra, its response
must be compared with physical energy, and its uniform estimate must survive
the asymptotically free continuum trajectory. The infimal trace additionally
needs a special complete-Dirichlet theorem. The available \(K_S\) applies
instead to a fixed-lattice strong-coupling region. Its Langevin parameter is
sampler time, not ontological order or reconstructed clock time.

The surviving stopping condition is therefore a typed regulator-uniform core
factorization. On a common complex energy-form core
\(\mathscr C_r\subset\mathcal H_r\), require

$$
D_r^{1/2}\Psi=C_rT_r\Psi
\quad(\Psi\in\mathscr C_r),
\qquad
\sup_r\|C_r\|<\infty,
$$

where \(D_r\) is the independently normalized categorical or Markov defect
on \(\mathcal H_r\),
\(T_r:\mathscr C_r\to\mathcal K_r\) is the physical modular/regional
response, and
\(C_r:\overline{T_r\mathscr C_r}\to\mathcal H_r\) is bounded. Together
with

$$
D_r\geq\kappa_{D,r}(I-P_{0,r}),
\qquad
\|T_r\Psi\|^2
\leq
\frac{2\pi R_r}{\hbar c}
\langle\Psi,H_r\Psi\rangle,
$$

it gives

$$
H_r
\geq
\frac{\hbar c}{2\pi R_r}
\frac{\kappa_{D,r}}{\|C_r\|^2}
(I-P_{0,r}).
$$

This is a quadratic-form inequality after closure from the common form core.
The full continuum stopping condition is

$$
\inf_r
\frac{\kappa_{D,r}}{R_r\|C_r\|^2}>0.
$$

Uniform dimensionless rigidity and factorization do not suffice if the
physical width \(R_r\) diverges.

This is the rigorous reversal tactic. The distinction operator is not
defined from \(H_r\); an algebraic process edge is first constructed and is
then proved to be visible to local relative entropy, which is bounded by
energy. The current open work is the natural Yang--Mills process action, its
net coherence and normalization, the whole-to-local relation, the physical
state-tangent comparison, the dimensional uniformity condition, and the
scale selector. [[modular-cocycle-tomography/inq|The
tomography theorem]] and [[kazhdan-markov-process-carrier|the process-carrier
theorem]] now own these two layers.

### The changing-carrier cocycle and the local-unitary correction

The complete vacuum-aligned matrix is useful only if it can survive changing
regulators, states, and channel resolutions without pretending that every
scale already acts on one Hilbert space. Let

$$
\Pi_k=I-P_{\Omega_k},
\qquad
\Pi_k=\sum_{a\in I_k}^{\mathrm s}Q_a^{(k)},
\qquad
P_k:\mathcal H_k\to\mathcal H_{k+1},
\qquad
P_k\Omega_k=\Omega_{k+1}.
$$

The centered arrows

$$
A_k=\Pi_{k+1}P_k\Pi_k
$$

compose exactly:

$$
\boxed{
A_{n:m}
=
A_{n-1}\cdots A_m
=
\Pi_nP_{n-1}\cdots P_m\Pi_m.}
\tag{DR81.1}
$$

The reason is purely algebraic:
$\Pi_{k+1}P_kP_{\Omega_k}=0$. If
$P_k^*\Omega_{k+1}=\Omega_k$, then $A_k$ is the restriction to the vacuum
complement; otherwise it is only the centered compression. For contractions,
the adjoint condition follows from forward vacuum preservation.

Choose bounded nonnegative matrices

$$
C_k(a,b)
\geq
\|Q_a^{(k+1)}P_kQ_b^{(k)}\|,
\qquad
C_k:\ell^2(I_k)\to\ell^2(I_{k+1}).
$$

For the block-magnitude vector
$r_k(x)_b=\|Q_b^{(k)}x\|$, orthogonality and the triangle inequality give

$$
r_{k+1}(A_kx)\leq C_kr_k(x)
$$

componentwise. Hence

$$
\boxed{
\|A_{n:m}\|
\leq
\|C_{n-1}\cdots C_m\|_{2\to2}.}
\tag{DR81.2}
$$

The same proof gives an entrywise bound for every endpoint block. Boundedness
of the scalar matrix is an additional analytic obligation: the direct sum of
normalized Walsh matrices is unitary, but the direct sum of its coordinate
block-norm matrices has unbounded norm.

If \(\mathscr B(P)\) is the exact matrix of block norms, then

$$
\boxed{
\mathscr B(RP)
\leq
\mathscr B(R)\mathscr B(P)}
\tag{DR81.3}
$$

entrywise. The operator-valued whole composes exactly; the scalar shadow is a
lax cocycle because it has forgotten the directions attaining the norms,
their phases, and cancellation between paths. For the normalized
two-dimensional Walsh matrix \(W\),

$$
W^2=I,
\qquad
\mathscr B(W^2)=I,
\qquad
\mathscr B(W)^2=
\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\1&1\end{pmatrix}.
$$

Thus the off-diagonal scalar paths survive even though they cancel exactly in
the whole operator. The nonnegative difference between the last two sides of
(DR81.3) is a scalarization defect, not entropy, causal charge, or mass.

The product theorem removes an unnecessary one-stage demand. On the centered
carrier let

$$
A_0=\operatorname{diag}(1,r),
\qquad
A_1=\operatorname{diag}(r,1),
\qquad
0<r<1.
$$

Both have norm one and both possess a nonvacuum fixed direction, but

$$
\boxed{A_1A_0=rI.}
\tag{DR81.4}
$$

Complementary stages can therefore be individually gapless while the joint
ordered product has a positive attenuation floor. This is the precise
multiplicative version of the paired-wall and opposed-causal-direction
intuition. It does not yet say that the product is a physical transfer.

For contractive stages, the product has an exact additive defect ledger.
Put

$$
B_0=I,
\qquad
B_k=A_{k-1}\cdots A_0,
\qquad
D_k=I-A_k^*A_k.
$$

Then

$$
\boxed{
I-B_n^*B_n
=
\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}B_k^*D_kB_k,}
\tag{DR81.4a}
$$

or, equivalently,

$$
\|x\|^2
=
\|B_nx\|^2
+
\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\|D_k^{1/2}B_kx\|^2.
\tag{DR81.4b}
$$

The stage defect acts on the carrier at that stage; the preceding product
pulls it back to the common initial carrier. Therefore

$$
\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}B_k^*D_kB_k
\geq\kappa I
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\|B_n\|\leq\sqrt{1-\kappa}.
\tag{DR81.4c}
$$

This is the exact global/local carrier statement: individual walls may miss
directions, while the transported family collectively forms a lower frame.
In infinite dimension, a trivial common kernel is not enough; the lower
frame must be uniform. The useful noncircular target is to derive wall maps
$L_k$ from Hessian, flux, or boundary geometry, prove
$D_k\geq L_k^*L_k$ on the actual transfer carrier, and prove a lower frame
for $L_kB_k$. Defining $L_k$ from the desired spectrum merely rewrites the
answer.

Projection also explains how an apparently stochastic or irreversible local
description may arise without putting dice into the ontology. Let
$U_k:\mathcal K_k\to\mathcal H_k$ select a visible subspace and put
$F_k=I-U_kU_k^*$. Then

$$
\boxed{
U_2^*P_1P_0U_0
=
(U_2^*P_1U_1)(U_1^*P_0U_0)
+
U_2^*P_1F_1P_0U_0.}
\tag{DR81.5}
$$

The last summand is an exact leave-and-return memory term. For one
self-adjoint transfer \(T\) and a visible projection \(E\),

$$
ET^2E=(ETE)^2+(FTE)^*(FTE).
\tag{DR81.6}
$$

The correction is positive. Mori's projection formalism develops the
continuous-time version into an exact generalized Langevin equation. Noise
appears only after the unresolved carrier is statistically represented or
approximated; the operator identity itself is deterministic.

This does not mean that leakage manufactures exact local unitarity. If \(T\)
is a contraction, \(U\) is an isometry, and \(U^*TU\) is unitary, equality in
the contraction estimate forces \(U\mathcal K\) to reduce \(T\). The local
unitary was already a noiseless sector. A genuinely nonfaithful formation
map can instead produce a quotient on which a kernel stabilizer acts
unitarily, and OS reconstruction can produce Lorentzian unitary evolution
from a positive Euclidean transfer. These are different arrows.

Changing faithful normal Type-III states adds a second exact separation.
Let \((M_s,\mathcal H_s,J_s,\mathcal P_s)\) be specified standard forms and
set \(\Omega_s=\xi_{\varphi_s}\in\mathcal P_s\). A normal isomorphism
\(\alpha_{t:s}:M_s\to M_t\) has Haagerup's unique
standard-form unitary \(U_{t:s}\). It maps the natural-cone vector of
\(\varphi_s\) to that of the pushed state

$$
\widehat\varphi_{t:s}
=
\varphi_s\circ\alpha_{t:s}^{-1},
$$

not to an independently chosen \(\varphi_t\). The residual real Connes
cocycle

$$
c_{t:s}(r)
=
[D\varphi_t:D\widehat\varphi_{t:s}]_r
\in\mathcal U(M_t)
$$

satisfies the exact horizontal law

$$
c_{u:s}(r)
=
c_{u:t}(r)\alpha_{u:t}(c_{t:s}(r)),
\tag{DR81.7}
$$

but the modular parameter \(r\) is not physical time and the real cocycle
does not in general carry the pushed natural-cone vector to the independently
chosen target vector. Under
\(\varphi_t\leq C\widehat\varphi_{t:s}\), Araki's bounded amplitude

$$
\mathsf R_{t:s}
=
A(\varphi_t/\widehat\varphi_{t:s}).
$$

The amplitude maps the pushed cone vector to \(\Omega_t\), realizes
\(\varphi_t(x)=\widehat\varphi_{t:s}(\mathsf R_{t:s}^*x\mathsf R_{t:s})\),
has squared norm equal to the sharp domination constant, and composes with
the transported
amplitudes. The pointed transport \(\mathsf R_{t:s}U_{t:s}\) is exact but
generally nonunitary. Under the corresponding two-sided domination
hypotheses, \(\mathsf R\) is boundedly invertible; only then does similarity transport
\(E\mapsto\mathsf R E\mathsf R^{-1}\) preserve orthogonality exactly when
\([\mathsf R^*\mathsf R,E]=0\). The correct construction is
therefore to transport raw channel data by the standard-form unitary and
recompute the vacuum-aligned internal and balance projections at the target
state. Diverging one-stage domination constants or incompatibility with the
channel endomorphism algebra obstruct this uniformly bounded,
similarity-based, channel-preserving Type-III lift; they do not rule out a
recomputed target resolution or a different composite construction.

Assign the stages declared positive physical thicknesses \(\ell_k\) and put
\(\ell_{n:m}=\sum_{k=m}^{n-1}\ell_k\). For a nonempty family of products of
one fixed thickness \(\ell_*>0\), put
\(\mathcal I_{\ell_*}=\{(m,n):m<n,\ell_{n:m}=\ell_*\}\neq\varnothing\).
The final stopping object is

$$
M_{n:m}=C_{n-1}\cdots C_m,
\qquad
\sup_{(m,n)\in\mathcal I_{\ell_*}}\|M_{n:m}\|_2
\leq q_*,
\qquad 0<q_*<1.
\tag{DR81.8}
$$

Its logarithm per slab is an inverse-length distinction-persistence rate,

$$
\gamma_{\mathrm{coc}}
=
-\frac{\log q_*}{\ell_*}.
\tag{DR81.9}
$$

Only if independently constructed unitary vacuum-aligning endpoint
identifications \(V_j:\mathcal H_j\to\mathcal K\) prove, for every
\((m,n)\in\mathcal I_{\ell_*}\),

$$
V_nA_{n:m}V_m^*
=
e^{-\ell_*(H-E_0)/(\hbar c)}(I-P_0)
\tag{DR81.10}
$$

does functional calculus yield

$$
\boxed{
\Delta_E
\geq
\hbar c\,\gamma_{\mathrm{coc}}
=
\frac{\hbar c}{\ell_*}\log\frac1{q_*}.}
\tag{DR81.11}
$$

Here one must define
\(P_0=\mathbf1_{\{0\}}(H-E_0)\) and
\(\Delta_E=\inf\sigma((H-E_0)|_{P_0^\perp\mathcal K})\), and require
\(P_0=P_\Omega\) for the unique-vacuum mass-gap statement. After
positive-energy Poincare reconstruction, define \(m_{\mathrm{gap}}\) as the
infimum of
\(c^{-2}\sqrt{E^2-c^2|\mathbf p|^2}\) over the joint spectrum on
\(P_0^\perp\mathcal K\).
Poincare covariance then gives
\(m_{\mathrm{gap}}=\Delta_E/c^2\) without assuming that the threshold is an
attained eigenstate. This is the exact content that survives
from “mass is a rate”: mass is the Poincare interpretation of a uniform
physical attenuation exponent, not the scalarization residue or the modular
metric distortion. The current Yang--Mills theorem target is to prove
(DR81.8) for the complete dual-charge, cycle-innovation, and balance
resolution across a fixed physical slab along the continuum trajectory, and
then prove (DR81.10).

### The transported response supplies the missing analysis map

The defect-frame theorem in (DR81.4a)--(DR81.4c) left one precise gap: an
analysis map $L_k$ had to arise from geometry without being defined from the
desired transfer spectrum. [[transported-response-observability-solder/inq|The
transported-response theorem]] now gives two exact constructions and one
uniform stopping condition.

For the general changing-carrier form, let

$$
A_k:\mathcal K_k\to\mathcal K_{k+1},
\qquad
\|A_k\|\leq1,
\qquad
D_k:=I_{\mathcal K_k}-A_k^*A_k,
\tag{DR82.1}
$$

and let $0\leq R_k\leq I_{\mathcal K_k}$ be an independently constructed
bounded response. A same-carrier comparison

$$
D_k\geq\eta_kR_k,
\qquad
\eta_k>0,
\tag{DR82.2}
$$

defines

$$
L_k:=\sqrt{\eta_k}R_k^{1/2},
\qquad
S_k:=D_k-L_k^*L_k\geq0.
\tag{DR82.3}
$$

Substitution into the exact telescoping identity gives the stronger typed
ledger

$$
\boxed{
I-B_{n:m}^*B_{n:m}
=
\sum_{k=m}^{n-1}B_{k:m}^*L_k^*L_kB_{k:m}
+
\sum_{k=m}^{n-1}B_{k:m}^*S_kB_{k:m}.}
\tag{DR82.4}
$$

Thus the transfer defect splits into the portion certified by the declared
geometry and a positive unsoldered remainder. This is an exact conservation-
shaped Hilbert-norm decomposition. It does not identify either term with
entropy, causal charge, factification, energy, or mass.

The first sum is the transported response Gramian

$$
\mathcal G_{n:m}^{\mathrm{resp}}
:=
\sum_{k=m}^{n-1}
\eta_kB_{k:m}^*R_kB_{k:m}.
\tag{DR82.5}
$$

It obeys

$$
0\leq\mathcal G_{n:m}^{\mathrm{resp}}
\leq I-B_{n:m}^*B_{n:m},
$$

so

$$
\boxed{
\mathcal G_{n:m}^{\mathrm{resp}}\geq\kappa I
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\|B_{n:m}\|\leq\sqrt{1-\kappa}.}
\tag{DR82.6}
$$

For a finite chain with positive weights,

$$
\boxed{
\ker\mathcal G_{n:m}^{\mathrm{resp}}
=
\bigcap_{k=m}^{n-1}
\ker(R_k^{1/2}B_{k:m}).}
\tag{DR82.7}
$$

This is the exact whole/part statement: a direction missed at one local
stage may be rotated or transported into a response detected at another.
In finite dimension, a trivial intersection yields a positive smallest
eigenvalue. In infinite dimension it yields only injectivity; closed range
or a quantitative lower-frame estimate is indispensable. An infinite-
horizon lower bound is also insufficient: the floor must occur uniformly
within a finite physical slab.

There is a canonical source of $L_k$ whenever the whole relation is already
given as two endpoint embeddings. Let

$$
J_k^-:\mathcal K_k\to\mathscr K_k,
\qquad
J_k^+:\mathcal K_{k+1}\to\mathscr K_k
$$

be isometries into one joint carrier and set

$$
A_k=(J_k^+)^*J_k^- ,
\qquad
L_k=(I-J_k^+(J_k^+)^*)J_k^- .
\tag{DR82.8}
$$

Then

$$
\boxed{I-A_k^*A_k=L_k^*L_k.}
\tag{DR82.9}
$$

The residual is literally the component of one endpoint presentation
orthogonal to the other inside the joint whole. Conditional transport of a
two-slice law and the Stinespring correspondence of a state-preserving UCP
map instantiate this identity without stochastic ontology. It is the
strongest exact pre-QFT $P_k/L_k$ type match in the workspace. Its current
obstruction is realization: the scale-correspondence prestack supplies bare
correspondences but not a coherent expectation or Q-system, pointed edge
state, endpoint isometries, regional naturality, or physical transfer
intertwiner. The core binary expectation supplies a concrete intra-fibre
readout but not a scale-composable physical wall.

The second route keeps physical transfer and geometry independent. Let
$G_k\geq0$ be a self-adjoint response generator and set

$$
R_k=I-e^{-\tau_kG_k},
\qquad
\ker R_k=\ker G_k.
\tag{DR82.10}
$$

This bounded transform is domain-safe even for unbounded $G_k$. The
parameter $\tau_k$ is response resolution, not physical time.

Boundedness is forced by the target rather than chosen for convenience. Since
$0\leq D_k\leq I$, any densely defined $L_k$ satisfying
$L_k^*L_k\leq D_k$ extends to an everywhere-defined contraction. Raw
unbounded gradients, precision square roots, Witten--Darboux factors, and
Dirac-type operators are therefore excluded as direct $L_k$ candidates.
Their heat defects or bounded resolvent windows may qualify. The
resolvent-logistic family
$Q_N(\widehat G)=(e^N\widehat G)^{1/2}(I+e^N\widehat G)^{-1}$ has norm at
most $1/2$ and the correct radical, but a physical window and a comparison
$D_k\geq w_kQ_{N_k}(\widehat G_k)^2$ remain unselected. Its full Parseval
integral is a support projection and is generally too large to assign to one
stage.

If the stage acts on one carrier and

$$
0\leq A_k\leq I,
\qquad
I-A_k\geq\eta_k(I-e^{-\tau_kG_k}),
\tag{DR82.11}
$$

then

$$
I-A_k^2
=(I-A_k)+A_k(I-A_k)
\geq I-A_k
\geq\eta_kR_k.
\tag{DR82.12}
$$

No commutation between $A_k$ and $R_k$ is needed. Squaring the order
inequality in (DR82.11) would be invalid because squaring is not operator
monotone. Positivity is a same-carrier hypothesis; for a rectangular
changing-carrier arrow the primitive statement must remain (DR82.2), unless
unitary identifications independently put the stage on one common positive
transfer carrier.

The workspace now contains three routes toward (DR82.11). Stationary
edge-measure domination gives an action-derived two-slice sufficient
certificate and is stable under tensor product, gauge restriction, and
deterministic blocking. Conditional-expectation disagreement gives
$G=I-E$ and
$I-e^{-\tau G}=(1-e^{-\tau})(I-E)$, but the noninjective expectation is an
analysis, not finite-depth physical transfer. A flux form or Hessian gives
$G$ only after it has been realized as a closed positive Hermitian form on
the complete physical Hilbert carrier. A parameter Hessian is natively
$T_\lambda M\to T_\lambda^*M$; it still needs a physical analysis map,
source norm or Riesz identification, and complex extension. Finite-rank
responses cannot cover an unrestricted infinite-dimensional carrier.

The complementary diagonal receipt makes the logical gain explicit. Take

$$
A_0=\operatorname{diag}(1,r),
\qquad
A_1=\operatorname{diag}(r,1),
$$

and complementary rank-one $G_0,G_1$. Each transfer has norm one and each
response has a nonvacuum kernel. Yet the transported response Gramian is
$(1-r)I$, so (DR82.6) gives the certified product bound $\sqrt r<1$; direct
multiplication gives the sharper product $rI$. The positive response
residual is $r(1-r)I$, and (DR82.4) recovers the exact total defect
$(1-r^2)I$. Geometry need not be falsely equated with the entire attenuation
in order to prove attenuation.

At fixed physical thickness $\ell_*$, a uniform floor
$\mathcal G_{n:m}^{\mathrm{resp}}\geq\kappa_*I$ gives

$$
\gamma_{\mathrm{resp}}
=
-\frac1{2\ell_*}\log(1-\kappa_*).
\tag{DR82.13}
$$

Only an independent OS-transfer identification converts this to
$\Delta_E\geq\hbar c\gamma_{\mathrm{resp}}$, and only Poincare-Casimir
reconstruction converts that energy edge to mass. The factor $1/2$ is
load-bearing because the Gramian controls squared norm.

This also gives the precise Copernican unitarity statement. The whole-to-
local formation may be a nonfaithful quotient or response radical; the
positive Euclidean transfer is a second arrow; and the reconstructed
Lorentzian unitary group is a third. A kernel stabilizer can become unitary
on the response quotient, and OS reconstruction can give a unitary group
from the positive generator. Faithful lossy compression cannot manufacture
exact unitarity: a unitary compression of a contraction is already a
reducing noiseless sector. One should therefore not predicate unitarity of
“the whole” as though a carrier were an arrow. The sharp cross-register
claim is that a primitive formation diagram need not be automorphic--and may
not yet admit a notion of unitarity--while a separately reconstructed clock
group can act unitarily on the selected physical carrier. Their connective
must be an intertwiner, not “so that.”

### Directed residue completion separates transfer completion from clock reversal

The preceding response theorem controls what the transfer loses, but leaves
the norm-completing carrier algebraically vague.
[[directed-isometric-residue-completion/inq|The directed-completion theorem]]
now supplies a minimal exact answer to that narrower question. For every
contraction

$$
A:\mathcal H\longrightarrow\mathcal K,
\qquad
\delta_A:=(I-A^*A)^{1/2},
\qquad
\mathcal D_A:=\overline{\operatorname{Ran}\delta_A},
\tag{DR83.1}
$$

the column

$$
\boxed{
V_Ax:=Ax\oplus\delta_Ax:
\mathcal H\longrightarrow\mathcal K\widehat\oplus\mathcal D_A}
\tag{DR83.2}
$$

is an isometry. It is onto exactly when $A$ is a coisometry:

$$
\boxed{V_A\text{ unitary}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad AA^*=I_{\mathcal K}.}
\tag{DR83.3}
$$

Consequently a nonidentity positive same-carrier transfer
$0\leq A\leq I$ has a proper non-surjective isometric completion. This is a
clean form of directedness which does not begin with a symmetry group and
then break it. Norm is preserved by the enlarged arrow, while only a proper
subset of final-plus-residue tuples are compatible histories. The theorem
does not yet prove absolute irreversibility: $V_A^*$ recovers an input on the
range, so operational irreversibility still requires a restriction on
admissible reverse maps or a stable physical record inclusion.
The isometry itself admits a further unitary dilation, and even an invertible
positive contraction has a proper defect column unless it is the identity.
Properness is therefore relative to the declared archive; this theorem does
not by itself construct pre-observable formation.

The defect carrier is universal rather than decorative. If
$W:\mathcal H\to\mathcal K\widehat\oplus\mathcal E$ is any isometry with
$P_{\mathcal K}W=A$, then there is a unique isometry
$U:\mathcal D_A\to\mathcal E$ onto the closure of the residual range such
that

$$
\boxed{W=(I_{\mathcal K}\oplus U)V_A.}
\tag{DR83.4}
$$

Thus $\mathcal D_A$ is the minimal norm-completing carrier determined by the
transfer. It is not thereby a physical environment, fact archive, or entropy
carrier. [[library/on-majorization-factorization-and-range-inclusion-of-operators-on-hilbert-space/inq|Douglas's
factorization theorem]] supplies the range/factorization tool used below.

For changing carriers, with
$D_k=I-A_k^*A_k$ and
$B_{k:m}=A_{k-1}\cdots A_m$, the complete finite arrow is

$$
\boxed{
V_{n:m}x
=B_{n:m}x
\oplus
\bigl(D_k^{1/2}B_{k:m}x\bigr)_{k=m}^{n-1},
\qquad
V_{n:m}^*V_{n:m}=I.}
\tag{DR83.5}
$$

The stage order remains visible because every residue is evaluated on the
transported input that actually reaches that stage. Under canonical
reassociation and permutation these columns compose chronologically: later stages
act on the survivor and earlier residue coordinates pass forward unchanged.
With minimal defect codomains the cascade is unitary exactly when every
$A_k$ is a coisometry. Positivity is load bearing if one wants defect,
attenuation, and direction to coincide: the unilateral shift is proper with
zero input defect, whereas the backward shift has nonzero input defect but a
unitary minimal column.

The full chronological archive in (DR83.5) is not generally the minimal
completion of the endpoint product. If

$$
\mathcal W_{n:m}x
:=\bigl(D_k^{1/2}B_{k:m}x\bigr)_k,
\qquad
\delta_B:=(I-B_{n:m}^*B_{n:m})^{1/2},
$$

then universal minimality gives an isometry from
$\overline{\operatorname{Ran}\delta_B}$ onto
$\overline{\operatorname{Ran}\mathcal W_{n:m}}$ through which
$\mathcal W_{n:m}$ factors. The larger direct sum can contain unreachable
coordinates that remember how the endpoint was decomposed into stages. Its
unitarity criterion is therefore stricter than the endpoint criterion
$B_{n:m}B_{n:m}^*=I$.

This finite changing-carrier column is an isometric completion, not a
Sz.-Nagy dilation. Only in the stationary case does the Schäffer isometry

$$
\widehat V_A(h,d_0,d_1,\ldots)
=(Ah,\delta_Ah,d_0,d_1,\ldots)
\tag{DR83.6}
$$

on
$\mathcal H\widehat\oplus\mathcal D_A\widehat\oplus\mathcal D_A\cdots$
obey

$$
P_{\mathcal H}\widehat V_A^j|_{\mathcal H}=A^j.
\tag{DR83.7}
$$

[[library/on-unitary-dilations-of-contractions/inq|Schäffer's original
matrix construction]] is the primary dilation precedent.

Its Wold decomposition separates a unitary part from a pure shift part. This
is a structural arrow, not yet a spectral gap. For $0\leq A\leq I$,

$$
A^j\xrightarrow{\mathrm s}P_1:=\mathbf1_{\{1\}}(A),
\tag{DR83.8}
$$

but the spectrum on $(I-P_1)\mathcal H$ may accumulate at $1$, giving
$A^jx\to0$ for every vector while $\|A^j(I-P_1)\|=1$ for every $j$. The
gap-strength statement is instead the uniform edge

$$
\boxed{
\|A|_{(I-P_1)\mathcal H}\|\leq q<1.}
\tag{DR83.9}
$$

The same distinction persists for an infinite nonstationary chain. The
decreasing operators $Q_n=B_{n:m}^*B_{n:m}$ have a strong limit $Q_\infty$
and

$$
I-Q_\infty
=\sum_{k=m}^{\infty}B_{k:m}^*D_kB_{k:m}
\tag{DR83.10}
$$

strongly. Even $Q_\infty=0$ states only pointwise eventual capture. It does
not furnish a uniform response floor in a bounded number of stages and is
therefore not a mass-gap theorem.

The response solder of DR82 refines rather than replaces the canonical
defect. If

$$
D_k=L_k^*L_k+S_k,
\qquad S_k\geq0,
\tag{DR83.11}
$$

then (DR83.5) splits isometrically into survivor, witnessed response, and
positive unsoldered residue. Douglas factorization gives the exact carrier
map

$$
\boxed{
L_k=C_kD_k^{1/2},
\qquad
C_k:\overline{\operatorname{Ran}D_k^{1/2}}
\longrightarrow\overline{\operatorname{Ran}L_k}
\text{ contractive}.}
\tag{DR83.12}
$$

The canonical transfer-defect carrier is therefore prior to the chosen
geometric readout. Equality $L_k^*L_k=D_k$ says that the readout accounts for
all defect norm; it still does not turn amplitude into an outcome without a
state and instrument.

For one positive stationary generator $K\geq0$, the discrete grammar has an
exact continuous-depth counterpart. Define

$$
T_s=e^{-sK},
\qquad
(\mathcal R_tx)(u)=\sqrt{2K}e^{-uK}x
\quad(0<u<t).
\tag{DR83.13}
$$

Then

$$
\boxed{
\mathcal R_t^*\mathcal R_t=I-e^{-2tK},
\qquad
x\longmapsto T_tx\oplus\mathcal R_tx
\text{ is isometric}.}
\tag{DR83.14}
$$

The residue-output densities concatenate by interval translation in the
ambient $L^2$ spaces. A stationary discrete partition maps exactly to
(DR83.13) on the image of the initial carrier; no mesh limit is needed for
that identity. The full archive codomains need not be isomorphic across
partitions, and this correlated additive concatenation is not a product
system or an $E_0$-semigroup. It also does not solve the continuum limit for
varying carriers and generators.
[[library/continuous-analogues-of-fock-space/inq|Arveson's construction]]
supplies the tensor-multiplicative product-system standard that would have to
be met before either name is earned.

On a declared $K$-reducing vacuum complement, with the first inequality
understood in quadratic-form sense,

$$
\boxed{
K\geq\Delta I
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\|\mathcal R_tx\|^2
\geq(1-e^{-2t\Delta})\|x\|^2
\text{ for every }t\geq0.}
\tag{DR83.15}
$$

Equivalently, one finite-depth floor
$\|\mathcal R_{t_*}x\|^2\geq\kappa\|x\|^2$ gives

$$
\boxed{
K\geq-\frac1{2t_*}\log(1-\kappa)I.}
\tag{DR83.16}
$$

This is the rigorous remainder of the phrase “mass is a rate”: before
physical reconstruction, $\Delta$ is the amplitude attenuation exponent and
$2\Delta$ is the minimum instantaneous squared-norm residue-production rate.
It becomes an energy or mass only after the same carrier is identified with
the reflection-positive Yang--Mills transfer carrier and then with the
positive-energy Poincare representation.

The resulting Copernican type diagram is

$$
\boxed{
V:\text{minimal isometric residue completion},
\qquad
T_s:\text{positive contraction semigroup},
\qquad
U_t:\text{reconstructed Lorentzian clock group}.}
\tag{DR83.17}
$$

These are not three names for one operator. When
$K=(H-E_0)/(\hbar c)$, let $U_t^{\mathrm{res}}$ act pointwise on the
ambient residue-output functions. Functional calculus gives the exact
intertwiner

$$
\boxed{
W_sU_t=(U_t\oplus U_t^{\mathrm{res}}|_{\mathcal D_s})W_s.}
\tag{DR83.18}
$$

Thus a proper Euclidean residue completion can be equivariant under a
distinct Lorentzian unitary clock; neither arrow creates the other. More
generally, a primitive formation diagram need not be an automorphism
diagram--and may not yet admit a notion of unitarity--while a selected
physical carrier may later admit unitary clock evolution. The valid theorem
is coexistence expressed by an intertwiner, not the claim that lost
information manufactures unitarity. The remaining physical work is to
construct a pointed Yang--Mills correspondence or action-derived response,
prove its coherent regional composition and complete-vacuum coverage, and
obtain a regulator-uniform lower frame over one fixed physical slab.

### Pointed fusion makes one kind of forgetting exact, while path innovations make the gap-bearing kind exact

The next Copernican step is to ask what the proposed descent operator
actually operates on.  There are two natural answers, and they are not the
same operator.

Let $A,B,C$ be von Neumann algebras and let

$$
\Phi:A\longrightarrow B,
\qquad
\Psi:B\longrightarrow C
\tag{DR84.1}
$$

be normal UCP maps.  Paschke's self-dual GNS correspondence $E_\Phi$ is the
completion of $A\odot B$ with

$$
\langle a\otimes b,a'\otimes b'\rangle_B
=b^*\Phi(a^*a')b',
\qquad
\xi_\Phi=[1\otimes1].
\tag{DR84.2}
$$

The composite does not generally give the whole fused correspondence.  There
is instead a canonical isometry

$$
\boxed{
v_{\Phi,\Psi}:E_{\Psi\Phi}\longrightarrow
E_\Phi\bar\otimes_BE_\Psi,
\qquad
v_{\Phi,\Psi}(a\xi_{\Psi\Phi}c)
=a\xi_\Phi\otimes\xi_\Psi c.}
\tag{DR84.3}
$$

The equality of inner products is exactly

$$
\begin{aligned}
&\left\langle
a\xi_\Phi\otimes\xi_\Psi c,
a'\xi_\Phi\otimes\xi_\Psi c'
\right\rangle_C\\
&\qquad=
c^*\Psi\!\left(\Phi(a^*a')\right)c'.
\end{aligned}
\tag{DR84.4}
$$

Its range is the self-dual cyclic subcorrespondence generated by
$\xi_\Phi\otimes\xi_\Psi$.  Self-duality is load bearing: it makes $v$
adjointable and its range orthogonally complemented.  Thus

$$
U_{\Phi,\Psi}:=v_{\Phi,\Psi}^*,
\qquad
r_{\mathrm{fus}}:=I-v_{\Phi,\Psi}v_{\Phi,\Psi}^*
\tag{DR84.5}
$$

give a coisometric endpoint descent and a fusion residue.  For every fused
history $z$,

$$
\boxed{
\langle z,z\rangle_C
=\langle U_{\Phi,\Psi}z,U_{\Phi,\Psi}z\rangle_C
+\langle r_{\mathrm{fus}}z,r_{\mathrm{fus}}z\rangle_C.}
\tag{DR84.6}
$$

This is a rigorous answer to one reading of “something is forgotten.”  The
composite CP map retains the cyclic endpoint direction and omits orthogonal
intermediate-factorization directions.  For a CP semigroup the maps (DR84.3)
associate coherently, while their adjoints give coisometric multiplication:
the pointwise GNS fibers form a subproduct system, not automatically a
product system.  A two-state depolarizing Markov semigroup already makes the
inclusion strict.  Each positive-time GNS fiber has four edge directions,
the tensor of two fibers has eight length-two path directions, and the
composite fiber again has four endpoint directions.  The four orthogonal
route contrasts are absent from the minimal composite.

Replacing $e^{-t}$ by $e^{-\varepsilon t}$ leaves that residue rank equal to
four for every $\varepsilon>0$, while the centered generator edge is
$\varepsilon\downarrow0$.  A nonzero fusion projection, its norm, and its
integer rank are therefore qualitative path-multiplicity data, not a lower
bound on a rate.  In the commutative path model the residue is precisely the
conditional-mean-zero middle-state or Markov-bridge carrier after both
endpoints are fixed.

This theorem also corrects the unitarity slogan.  A carrier is not unitary
or nonunitary; those are predicates of arrows on a specified carrier.  The
descent $U_{\Phi,\Psi}$ may be a proper coisometry while an adjointable
unitary $q$ acts on the retained composite correspondence.  In fact

$$
\widetilde q
=v_{\Phi,\Psi}qv_{\Phi,\Psi}^*+r_{\mathrm{fus}}
\tag{DR84.7}
$$

is a unitary on the fused carrier and satisfies
$U_{\Phi,\Psi}\widetilde q=qU_{\Phi,\Psi}$.  Local unitary grammar can
therefore coexist with noninvertible descent.  The residue does not cause
the unitary; the exact statement is a quotient intertwiner.  This is the
operator-algebraic version of putting the explanatory order in the right
place.

The same theorem contains a no-go that prevents premature promotion to a
mass gap:

$$
\boxed{r_{\mathrm{fus}}v_{\Phi,\Psi}=0.}
\tag{DR84.8}
$$

The canonical lift of an endpoint distinction lies wholly inside the cyclic
range, so pulling the fusion residue back along that lift gives zero.  A
proper GNS fusion inclusion is consequently not a positive response on the
physical endpoint carrier.  A separate history-sensitive realization must
enter the noncyclic directions.

The stationary path construction supplies a different residue with exactly
the gap-bearing type.  Let $(X_k)$ be a stationary Markov chain with invariant
law $\nu$, let $P$ be its forward contraction on $L^2(\nu)$, and put

$$
\mathcal F_k:=\sigma(X_k,X_{k+1},\ldots),
\qquad
\mathsf E_k:=\mathbb E[\,\cdot\mid\mathcal F_k].
\tag{DR84.9}
$$

For $J_kf=f(X_k)$, stationarity and the Markov property give

$$
\mathsf E_kJ_0f=J_k(P^*)^kf.
\tag{DR84.10}
$$

Reversibility is precisely the hypothesis that replaces backward prediction
$P^*$ by the same $P$ used for forward transfer.  In that case define

$$
\Delta_k^f
:=(\mathsf E_k-\mathsf E_{k+1})J_0f
=(P^kf)(X_k)-(P^{k+1}f)(X_{k+1}).
\tag{DR84.11}
$$

The differences of the nested expectation projections are mutually
orthogonal.  Hence

$$
\boxed{
f(X_0)
=(P^nf)(X_n)+\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\Delta_k^f,}
\qquad
\boxed{
\|f\|^2
=\|P^nf\|^2+\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\|\Delta_k^f\|^2.}
\tag{DR84.12}
$$

At the operator level,

$$
\boxed{
J_0^*(\mathsf E_k-\mathsf E_{k+1})J_0
=P^k(I-P^2)P^k,}
\qquad
\boxed{
J_0^*(\mathsf E_0-\mathsf E_n)J_0
=I-P^{2n}.}
\tag{DR84.13}
$$

Thus the abstract stage defect in DR83 is now realized inside one classical
path carrier as an orthogonal conditional-prediction innovation.  No claim
of stochastic ontology follows.  The probability law represents what a
local presentation can condition on; the construction is compatible with a
globally necessitating structure whose antecedent cannot be inverted from
that presentation.

The distinctions are now exact:

| object | carrier | exact meaning |
|---|---|---|
| $r_{\mathrm{fus}}$ | fused $W^*$-correspondence | intermediate route contrasts omitted by the minimal composite CP correspondence |
| $\mathsf E_k-\mathsf E_{k+1}$ | full stationary path $L^2$ | the new loss in predicting the initial distinction from a more remote future |
| $I-P^{2n}$ | physical slice $L^2(\nu)$ | squared-norm transfer defect across the slab |

The second pulls back to the third by (DR84.13).  No canonical map identifies
the first with either of them.  [[contemporary-puzzles/yang-mills-mass-gap/fusion-residue-is-not-transfer-defect|The
fusion/transfer firewall]] states the required comparison without collapsing
these types.

The finite Wilson transfer provides a regulated instantiation.  If $T$ is a
strictly positive self-adjoint transfer with Perron pair
$T\psi=\lambda_0\psi$, the Doob transform $P_T$ has invariant law
$\nu_T=\psi^2\mu$.  Infinite outer vacuum preparation with a fixed inner
separation $d$ gives the two-cut law

$$
\boxed{
\mathrm d\mathsf J_T^{(d)}(U,V)
=\lambda_0^{-d}\psi(U)K_{T^d}(U,V)\psi(V)
\,\mathrm d\mu(U)\mathrm d\mu(V)
=\nu_T(\mathrm dU)P_T^d(U,\mathrm dV).}
\tag{DR84.14}
$$

With $J_-f=f(U)$, $J_+g=g(V)$ and reversibility,
$J_+^*J_-=P_T^d$.  Therefore

$$
L_d:=(I-J_+J_+^*)J_-,
\qquad
(L_df)(U,V)=f(U)-P_T^df(V),
$$

$$
\boxed{L_d^*L_d=I-P_T^{2d}.}
\tag{DR84.15}
$$

This vacuum-prepared fixed-inner-slab law must not be replaced by the finite
periodic two-cut Hilbert--Schmidt law proportional to $|K_n(U,V)|^2$; their
outer preparations and limiting correlations differ.  Nor does reflection
positivity alone identify an OS interface carrier with the Doob slice
carrier.  Those identifications remain separate gluing theorems.

On the complete vacuum complement, define
$\mathfrak I_n(f)=\sum_{k<n}\|\Delta_k^f\|^2$.  Then

$$
\boxed{
\inf_{\|f\|=1}\mathfrak I_n(f)
=1-\|P^n\|^2.}
\tag{DR84.16}
$$

If $P=e^{-a(H-E_0)/(\hbar c)}$ and $na=\ell$, a lower frame
$\mathfrak I_n(f)\geq\kappa\|f\|^2$ is equivalent to

$$
\boxed{
\Delta_E
\geq-\frac{\hbar c}{2\ell}\log(1-\kappa).}
\tag{DR84.17}
$$

The factor $1/2$ is forced because the innovation ledger measures squared
norm.  Positivity of the transfer is also load bearing: without it the
criterion controls the absolute spectral radius and a reversible $-1$ mode
can evade every innovation despite an upper-edge Poincare gap.

The new construction therefore supplies a carrier, a precise forgetting
operator, and the exact stopping condition, but not the missing estimate.
To make categorical fusion independently explain the transfer gap, one must
construct a bounded history-sensitive analysis $\Gamma_{a,L}$ into a
represented fused correspondence and prove, on every centered
gauge-invariant direction,

$$
\boxed{
\kappa_*I
\leq
\Gamma_{a,L}^*r_{\mathrm{fus},a,L}\Gamma_{a,L}
\leq
I-P_{a,L}^{2n(a)},
\qquad
n(a)a_{\tau,a}\longrightarrow\ell_*>0,}
\tag{DR84.18}
$$

uniformly in spatial volume, admissible boundary or flux sector, and
regulator removal.  The left inequality is the new geometric coercivity
theorem; the right is the physical carrier solder.  The canonical cyclic
lift cannot be $\Gamma$ by (DR84.8).  Alternatively, the reverse-prediction
archive gives the right equality directly, reducing the remaining task to a
uniform Yang--Mills innovation estimate for the actual vacuum-prepared path
law.  Only after this estimate, OS reconstruction, and the Poincare Casimir
identification does the dimensionless distinction floor acquire the units
of mass.

## DR85. Middle insertion turns bridge ambiguity into a transfer-dominated response

DR84 found an exact fusion residue but also proved that the canonical
endpoint lift cannot see it. The missing map is supplied by the middle of the
path, not by another endpoint construction. Let $(X_j)_{j\in\mathbb Z}$ be a
stationary Markov path on a standard Borel state space, with invariant law
\(\nu\), forward contraction

$$
(Pf)(x)=\mathbb E[f(X_1)\mid X_0=x],
\qquad
J_jf=f(X_j),
\tag{DR85.1}
$$

and endpoint expectation

$$
\mathsf E_{0,2n}
:=
\mathbb E[\,\cdot\mid\sigma(X_0,X_{2n})],
\qquad
r_n:=I-\mathsf E_{0,2n}.
\tag{DR85.2}
$$

In the scalarized commutative pointed-GNS fusion, the range of the composite
cyclic inclusion is exactly the endpoint-measurable subspace. Hence $r_n$
is the represented fusion-residue projection. The canonical
history-sensitive analysis is

$$
\boxed{
L_n^{\mathrm{br}}
:=r_nJ_n,
\qquad
B_n^{\mathrm{br}}
:=(L_n^{\mathrm{br}})^*L_n^{\mathrm{br}}
=J_n^*r_nJ_n.}
\tag{DR85.3}
$$

The operator operates on arbitrary middle-slice observables in $L^2(\nu)$,
not on mass, a finite parameter list, or the endpoint correspondence. Its
quadratic form is

$$
\boxed{
\langle f,B_n^{\mathrm{br}}f\rangle
=
\mathbb E\!\left[
\operatorname{Var}
\left(f(X_n)\mid X_0,X_{2n}\right)
\right].}
\tag{DR85.4}
$$

This is the portion of a middle distinction not reconstructible from both
bounding presentations. It is a projection-theoretic statement, not a claim
that the represented path is ontologically stochastic.

Because conditioning on both endpoints contains conditioning on either one,

$$
\mathsf E_0\leq\mathsf E_{0,2n},
\qquad
\mathsf E_{2n}\leq\mathsf E_{0,2n}.
\tag{DR85.5}
$$

The Markov property gives

$$
\mathsf E_0J_n=J_0P^n,
\qquad
\mathsf E_{2n}J_n=J_{2n}(P^*)^n.
\tag{DR85.6}
$$

Nested projection order therefore proves the two exact upper solders

$$
\boxed{
0\leq B_n^{\mathrm{br}}
\leq I-(P^*)^nP^n,
\qquad
B_n^{\mathrm{br}}
\leq I-P^n(P^*)^n.}
\tag{DR85.7}
$$

For reversible transfer $P=P^*$,

$$
\boxed{0\leq B_n^{\mathrm{br}}\leq I-P^{2n}.}
\tag{DR85.8}
$$

This closes the map and upper-comparison parts of DR84.18 with
\(\Gamma=J_n\). It does not prove a lower edge.

The lower edge itself has an exact geometric meaning. For any orthogonal
slice projection $Q$, define

$$
\rho_n^{\mathrm{br}}(Q)
:=
\|\mathsf E_{0,2n}J_nQ\|.
\tag{DR85.8a}
$$

Since $J_n$ is an isometry and $\mathsf E_{0,2n}$ is an orthogonal
projection,

$$
\boxed{
\inf_{\substack{f\in\operatorname{Ran}Q\\\|f\|=1}}
\langle f,B_n^{\mathrm{br}}f\rangle
=
1-\rho_n^{\mathrm{br}}(Q)^2.}
\tag{DR85.8b}
$$

Thus a bridge lower frame is precisely a positive subspace angle, or
equivalently a uniform ceiling on how well the two boundary slices can
jointly recover a normalized middle-slice distinction. This is a
two-ended maximal-correlation statement, not ordinary one-ended transfer
correlation.

The same operator is the Fisher face of bridge ambiguity. Disintegrate the
three-slice law over its endpoints and write

$$
\beta_n^{x,z}(\mathrm dy)
=
\mathbb P(X_n\in\mathrm dy\mid X_0=x,X_{2n}=z).
\tag{DR85.9}
$$

For a bounded real middle source $f$, the endpoint-preserving fiber tilt

$$
\frac{\mathrm d\beta_{n,\theta f}^{x,z}}
     {\mathrm d\beta_n^{x,z}}(y)
=
\frac{e^{\theta f(y)}}
     {\int e^{\theta f}\,\mathrm d\beta_n^{x,z}}
\tag{DR85.10}
$$

has score at zero

$$
f(X_n)-\mathbb E[f(X_n)\mid X_0,X_{2n}]
=L_n^{\mathrm{br}}f.
\tag{DR85.11}
$$

Thus $B_n^{\mathrm{br}}$ is the global score Fisher Gramian. In a fixed
dominating-measure trivialization, the derivative of the normalized bridge
half-density is one half of that score, so its GNS Hilbert Gramian is
\(B_n^{\mathrm{br}}/4\). A globally normalized path tilt has a different
score; the endpoint-preserving fiber normalization in (DR85.10) is load
bearing.

For positive raw kernels $k_n$ and a common Doob function $h$,

$$
p_n^h(x,y)
=
\frac{k_n(x,y)h(y)}{\lambda_nh(x)},
$$

the conditional bridge obeys

$$
\boxed{
\frac{p_n^h(x,y)p_n^h(y,z)}{p_{2n}^h(x,z)}
=
\frac{k_n(x,y)k_n(y,z)}{k_{2n}(x,z)}.}
\tag{DR85.12}
$$

The Perron vector and eigenvalue cancel fiberwise. In finite Wilson theory,
the middle bridge is consequently determined by the raw transfer kernel and
middle action weight. The endpoint base law used to integrate (DR85.4) still
contains the Perron vector, so this cancellation does not solve the global
vacuum problem. It does, however, expose a pointwise action-derived object on
which geometric kernel or conditional-variance estimates can be attempted.

Let $Q:=I-\Pi_{\operatorname{Fix}(P)}$ be the complete nonfixed projection.
The new stopping condition is

$$
\boxed{
B_{n(a),a,L,\mathsf s}^{\mathrm{br}}
\geq\kappa_*Q_{a,L,\mathsf s},
\qquad
\kappa_*>0,
\qquad
n(a)a_{\tau,a}\longrightarrow\ell_*>0,}
\tag{DR85.13}
$$

uniformly in volume, admissible sector or boundary data, and regulator
removal. Combining (DR85.8) and (DR85.13) gives

$$
\|P^{n(a)}Q\|\leq\sqrt{1-\kappa_*},
\qquad
\Delta_E
\geq
-\frac{\hbar c}{2\ell_*}\log(1-\kappa_*).
\tag{DR85.14}
$$

Only after Poincare reconstruction may the last bound be divided by $c^2$
and called a mass bound. The dimensionless datum is the bridge-distinction
fraction $\kappa_*$; $\ell_*,c,\hbar$ are downstream calibration data.

One concrete sufficient criterion is a uniform bridge minorization. If

$$
\beta_n^{x,z}\geq\varepsilon\nu
\quad\text{for endpoint-almost every }(x,z),
\tag{DR85.15}
$$

then

$$
\operatorname{Var}_{\beta_n^{x,z}}(f)
\geq\varepsilon\operatorname{Var}_\nu(f),
\qquad
B_n^{\mathrm{br}}\geq\varepsilon(I-\Pi_{\mathbb C1}).
\tag{DR85.16}
$$

This follows by writing
\(\beta_n^{x,z}=\varepsilon\nu+(1-\varepsilon)\gamma^{x,z}\) and minimizing
the squared distance over constants. Fixed finite strictly positive kernels
may supply some $\varepsilon>0$, but such a constant commonly collapses
with volume or regulator removal; it is not a continuum proof.

The bridge floor is sufficient, not generally equivalent, to a transfer gap.
Its exact kernel consists of those $f$ for which $f(X_n)$ is determined by
the two endpoints. A reversible period-two flip leaves its alternating mode
in this kernel and also lacks a positive Hamiltonian logarithm. In the
nonreversible shift-register chain $X_k=(\xi_k,\xi_{k+1})$, two steps erase
centered transfer memory while the midpoint remains determined by the two
endpoints, so the bridge form can vanish despite strong transfer
contraction. Positivity of a finite kernel proves at most qualitative
kernel removal. The regulator-uniform lower frame in (DR85.13), gauge-safe
carrier reduction, OS interface identification, continuum construction, and
Poincare realization remain the proof obligations.

Finally, this theorem makes the unitarity clue exact without reversing its
logic. Endpoint conditioning and the GNS endpoint map are projections or
coisometries, not clock evolutions. A separately reconstructed unitary may
act on the retained endpoint carrier and intertwine with descent. The bridge
residue and $L_n^{\mathrm{br}}$ say what the endpoint presentation omits;
they do not manufacture the local unitary or make “the whole” an object to
which the untyped predicate *nonunitary* applies.

## DR86. The bridge floor is a canonical data-augmentation gap

The lower frame left open by DR85 is already the defect of a positive
Markov operator. Put \(Z=(X_0,X_{2n})\), let
\(\mathsf R_n=\operatorname{Law}(X_n,Z)\), and identify endpoint-measurable
functions with \(L^2(\mathsf J_{0,2n})\). The conditional transport

$$
\begin{aligned}
K_n &:L^2(\nu)\longrightarrow L^2(\mathsf J_{0,2n}),\\
(K_nf)(x,z)
&=
\mathbb E[f(X_n)\mid X_0=x,X_{2n}=z]
\end{aligned}
\tag{DR86.1}
$$

gives the self-adjoint Hilbert-positive Markov contraction

$$
\boxed{
S_n:=K_n^*K_n,
\qquad
B_n^{\mathrm{br}}=I-S_n.}
\tag{DR86.2}
$$

Disintegrate

$$
\mathsf R_n(\mathrm dy,\mathrm dz)
=
\nu(\mathrm dy)\alpha_y(\mathrm dz)
=
\mathsf J_{0,2n}(\mathrm dz)\beta_z(\mathrm dy).
\tag{DR86.3}
$$

Then \(S_n\) alternates from a middle value to a compatible endpoint pair
and back:

$$
S_n(y,\mathrm dy')
=
\int\alpha_y(\mathrm dz)\beta_z(\mathrm dy').
\tag{DR86.4}
$$

Its symmetric stationary edge law is

$$
\boxed{
\mathsf M_n(\mathrm dy,\mathrm dy')
=
\int
\beta_z(\mathrm dy)\beta_z(\mathrm dy')
\,\mathsf J_{0,2n}(\mathrm dz).}
\tag{DR86.5}
$$

This is exactly the marginal chain of a two-component Gibbs or
data-augmentation update. Liu, Wong, and Kong's operator analysis identifies
the corresponding marginal convergence coefficient with squared maximal
correlation. Here that standard theorem acquires a physical solder because
DR85 already proved

$$
\boxed{
P^{2n}\leq S_n\leq I}
\tag{DR86.6}
$$

for reversible \(P\). The auxiliary chain is not physical time evolution;
its defect is a separately typed response already ordered below the
physical round-trip defect.

The fixed space says exactly what would defeat the construction:

$$
\boxed{
\ker(I-S_n)
=
\{f:f(X_n)=g(X_0,X_{2n})\text{ a.s. for some }g\}.}
\tag{DR86.7}
$$

A nonconstant element is a perfectly endpoint-recoverable middle
distinction. In a finite or compact conditional-transport problem, absence
of such a common factor gives a strict finite-system angle. It gives no
uniform thermodynamic or continuum angle, because the centered norm may
approach one without attaining it.

The first useful economy is tensorization. For independent
middle--boundary pairs with centered maximal correlations \(\rho_i\),
Witsenhausen's theorem gives

$$
\boxed{
\rho_{\mathrm{product}}=\max_i\rho_i,
\qquad
\kappa_{\mathrm{product}}
=
\min_i(1-\rho_i^2).}
\tag{DR86.8}
$$

Thus product link bridges do not multiply one minorization loss per link.
If each one-link bridge satisfies
\(\beta_{i,z_i}\geq\varepsilon_i\nu_i\), then the global bridge floor is at
least \(\min_i\varepsilon_i\), even though direct product minorization
retains only \(\prod_i\varepsilon_i\). Restriction to a gauge-invariant
subspace cannot worsen a form inequality already proved upstairs, provided
the path law and conditional transport commute with the gauge action. This
gives a volume-uniform finite-regulator bridge floor for the pure product
kinetic member; it does not control its weak-coupling continuum rate.

For the interacting Perron law, choose complete middle and endpoint-pair
innovations

$$
Q_M=\sum_iD_i^M,
\qquad
Q_\partial=\sum_jD_j^\partial,
\qquad
\|D_j^\partial K_nD_i^M\|\leq c_{ji}.
\tag{DR86.9}
$$

With \(C=(c_{ji})\), the block-operator estimate gives

$$
\boxed{
I-S_n
\geq
\left(1-\|C\|_{2\to2}^2\right)Q_M.}
\tag{DR86.10}
$$

The resolution must contain the gauge-cycle, dual-boundary-charge, and
vacuum-balance sectors. Separate regional gauge closure or internal block
centering can delete precisely the collective mode whose norm is close to
one.

There is a second noncircular route. Let \(D\geq0\) independently generate
a conservative reversible Markov semigroup on the same vacuum carrier,
\(Q_\tau=e^{-\tau D}\). If the off-diagonal stationary edge measures obey

$$
\mathsf M_n^\circ
\geq
\eta\,\mathsf J_{Q_\tau}^\circ,
\qquad
\eta>0,
\tag{DR86.11}
$$

then the edge-measure solder proves

$$
\boxed{
I-S_n
\geq
\eta(I-e^{-\tau D}).}
\tag{DR86.12}
$$

If \(D\geq\lambda_DQ_M\), this yields

$$
\kappa_n
\geq
\eta(1-e^{-\tau\lambda_D}).
\tag{DR86.13}
$$

Conditional Poincare and Dobrushin estimates for the actual one-slice
vacuum law may bound \(\lambda_D\). They cannot supply \(\eta\). On the
uniform product bit law, ordinary one-site heat-bath mixing is perfect, but
the positive parity transfer with density

$$
p_a(x,y)=1+a\chi(x)\chi(y)
\tag{DR86.14}
$$

has bridge floor

$$
\frac{1-a^2}{1+a^2}
\longrightarrow0
\qquad
(a\uparrow1).
\tag{DR86.15}
$$

This proves that a one-slice functional inequality alone has the wrong
logical type. The temporal edge comparison (DR86.11), or the complete
bridge matrix (DR86.10), is load bearing.

At fixed physical half-slab, the sharpened Wilson stopping condition is

$$
\boxed{
\sup_{\substack{a<a_0,\ L,\ \mathsf s}}
\|C_{a,L,\mathsf s}^{\mathrm{br}}(\ell_*)\|_2
\leq q_*<1,}
\tag{DR86.16}
$$

or an edge-measure estimate producing the same lower frame. It implies

$$
\Delta_E
\geq
-\frac{\hbar c}{\ell_*}\log q_*.
\tag{DR86.17}
$$

For an adjacent bridge, a finite physical energy instead requires its
dimensionless floor to vanish at the appropriate \(a_\tau\) rate; a fixed
positive adjacent-slice floor would represent a divergent energy. At fixed
physical \(\ell_*\), (DR86.16) is the clean uniform target.

This advances the Copernican programme without identifying measurement,
sampling, and clock evolution. The primitive object is the whole
middle--boundary relation. Its alternating conditional presentations
define \(S_n\); their failure to recover a middle distinction defines
\(I-S_n\). Only the independently proved order (DR86.6) lets that
dimensionless distinction defect constrain Euclidean attenuation. OS and
Poincare reconstruction then produce the Lorentzian unitary and mass
interpretation on a later carrier.

## DR87. The Gaussian bridge angle is exactly the inverse Compton rate

The free Gaussian theory gives a complete calibration of the bridge
operator. Let a normalized stationary mode have Euclidean covariance

$$
\mathbb E[X_sX_t]=e^{-\omega|s-t|},
\qquad
\omega>0,
\tag{DR87.1}
$$

and place the endpoints at \(-\ell,+\ell\) around the middle
\(Y=X_0\). Set \(r=e^{-\omega\ell}\) and
\(Z=(X_{-\ell},X_{+\ell})\). Then

$$
\operatorname{Cov}(Z)
=
\begin{pmatrix}1&r^2\\r^2&1\end{pmatrix},
\qquad
\operatorname{Cov}(Y,Z)=(r,r).
\tag{DR87.2}
$$

Gaussian regression gives

$$
\mathbb E[Y\mid Z]
=
\frac{r}{1+r^2}(X_{-\ell}+X_{+\ell})
\tag{DR87.3}
$$

and

$$
q^2
:=
\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb E[Y\mid Z])
=
\frac{2r^2}{1+r^2}.
\tag{DR87.4}
$$

The residual linear variance is therefore

$$
1-q^2
=
\frac{1-r^2}{1+r^2}
=
\tanh(\omega\ell).
\tag{DR87.5}
$$

Gaussianity makes this a full-\(L^2\) theorem rather than a linear
regression estimate. Normalize
\(W=q^{-1}\mathbb E[Y\mid Z]\). The Gaussian residual is independent of
the whole endpoint pair, and for normalized Hermite polynomials,

$$
K_\ell h_k(Y)
=
\mathbb E[h_k(Y)\mid Z]
=
q^kh_k(W).
\tag{DR87.6}
$$

Hence the data-augmentation return \(S_\ell=K_\ell^*K_\ell\) has
eigenvalues \(q^{2k}\) and the bridge Gramian has eigenvalues
\(1-q^{2k}\). The first chaos is sharp:

$$
\boxed{
B_\ell^{\mathrm{br}}
\geq
\tanh(\omega\ell)Q.}
\tag{DR87.7}
$$

This is the precise free-theory realization of the rate proposal:

$$
\boxed{
\omega
=
\ell^{-1}\operatorname{artanh}
\kappa_{\mathrm{br}}(\ell).}
\tag{DR87.8}
$$

The identity uses the optimal Gaussian floor. A certified lower bound
\(\kappa_{\rm cert}\leq\kappa_{\rm br}\) gives only
\(\omega\geq\ell^{-1}\operatorname{artanh}\kappa_{\rm cert}\).

For independent free-field modes with one-particle inverse-length
frequencies \(\Omega(p)\), Witsenhausen tensorization or Gaussian second
quantization gives

$$
\boxed{
\kappa_{\mathrm{field}}(\ell)
=
\tanh\!\left(
\ell\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_p\Omega(p)
\right).}
\tag{DR87.9}
$$

Thus arbitrarily soft modes force the bridge angle to zero. A massive free
field with \(\Omega(p)=\sqrt{|p|^2+\mu^2}\) has
\(\kappa=\tanh(\mu\ell)\). With \(\ell=c\tau\),

$$
\Gamma
=
\frac{1}{\tau}\operatorname{artanh}\kappa,
\qquad
\Delta_E=\hbar\Gamma,
\qquad
M=\frac{\hbar}{c^2}\Gamma
=
\frac{\hbar}{c\ell}\operatorname{artanh}\kappa.
\tag{DR87.10}
$$

The bridge floor is therefore a dimensionless rate coordinate in the
Gaussian theory. It is not a pixel or shortest observation length. The
dimensional input is the physical slab duration or length; neither that
yardstick nor \(\hbar,c\) has been derived.

Two firewalls are essential. First, the general bridge solder yields only

$$
\Delta_E
\geq
-\frac{\hbar c}{2\ell}
\log(1-\kappa),
\tag{DR87.11}
$$

not the Gaussian artanh equality. Second,
\(B_\ell^{\mathrm{br}}\neq\tanh(\ell H/(\hbar c))\) on higher chaoses even
though their lowest nonvacuum edges agree. Equation (DR87.8) reconstructs
the spectral bottom from the optimal floor; it is not a universal operator
functional calculus.

The Copernican reversal is now exact in one model. Instead of starting with
a mass eigenvalue and predicting exponential correlations, start with the
whole three-slice relation, remove everything about the middle that both
boundaries can present, and read the spectral rate from the irreducible
residue. Conditional expectation is a noninvertible presentation arrow;
the Lorentzian unitary appears only after reflection-positive
reconstruction on a later physical carrier.

## DR88. Whole-slab factorization is a direct interacting bridge theorem

Let a finite Euclidean path law be carried by the product of an initial
boundary block \(V_-\), the entire open slab \(V_0\), and a final boundary
block \(V_+\). For any block \(A\), write
\(\operatorname{Var}_A(F)\) for conditional variance when the variables in
\(A\) are resampled with the complement held fixed. Suppose

$$
\operatorname{Var}_\mu(F)
\leq
C_{\mathrm{BF}}
\sum_{A\in\{-,0,+\}}
\mathbb E_\mu[\operatorname{Var}_A(F)].
\tag{DR88.1}
$$

For \(F=f(X_n)\) supported at the midpoint, the two boundary terms vanish.
The interior term conditions on exactly the two endpoints. Therefore

$$
\boxed{
\operatorname{Var}_\nu(f)
\leq
C_{\mathrm{BF}}
\mathbb E\!\left[
\operatorname{Var}
\bigl(f(X_n)\mid X_0,X_{2n}\bigr)
\right],}
\tag{DR88.2}
$$

or

$$
\boxed{
B_n^{\mathrm{br}}
\geq
C_{\mathrm{BF}}^{-1}Q.}
\tag{DR88.3}
$$

This elementary specialization supplies the cleanest current route from
local statistical-mechanical control to the physical bridge: prove a
functional inequality for the **whole spacetime law**, then test it on the
local middle carrier. It does not replace physical time by a heat-bath
sampler.

Caputo and Parisi prove weighted block entropy factorization from strong
spatial mixing for finite subsets of \(\mathbb Z^d\) with product a priori
spin measure, bounded measurable nearest-neighbor pair and site potentials,
and their stated irreducibility condition when hard constraints are present;
the estimate is uniform in volume and external boundary condition on their
admissible family. Linearizing their entropy inequality first on bounded
observables and then extending by truncation gives (DR88.1), and their
arbitrary block weights permit the three-block partition directly because
its unit-weight coverage factor is one. A more explicit but typically more
restrictive pilot follows from
the coordinatewise heterogeneous-space extension of Wu's Wasserstein
Dobrushin proof. If \(\mathcal C\) is the interdependence matrix of the three
macroblock conditional laws, the required regular conditional distributions
and finite second moments exist, and
\(r_{\rm sp}(\mathcal C)<1\), then

$$
\boxed{
B_n^{\mathrm{br}}
\geq
\bigl(1-r_{\rm sp}(\mathcal C)\bigr)Q.}
\tag{DR88.4}
$$

This does not contradict the one-slice sampler no-go. A Dobrushin matrix for
the one-slice marginal concerns a different law and a different operator.
The matrix in (DR88.4) belongs to the full initial--interior--final
specification, so its only surviving block form on a midpoint observable is
already the temporal bridge variance.

The same object has a channel formulation. Let
\(\mathcal B_n:X_n\mapsto(X_0,X_{2n})\) be the endpoint channel. Raginsky's
fixed-input channel Poincare identity gives

$$
\boxed{
\kappa_{\mathcal H}^{\mathrm{br}}
=
1-\eta_{\chi^2,\mathcal H}(\nu,\mathcal B_n)
=
1-\|K_n|_{\mathcal H}\|^2.}
\tag{DR88.5}
$$

This identifies the exact coefficient but does not estimate it. Assuming a
uniform channel SDPI is simply another statement of the target unless its
constant is derived from the action, a spatial-mixing theorem, or a
scale-controlled RG construction.

The additional hypothesis cannot be omitted. Along admissible LPS degrees
\(d=p+1\to\infty\), with auxiliary parameters chosen to obtain the
bipartite high-girth branch, let \(G_d=(L_d,R_d,E_d)\) be the resulting
connected \(d\)-regular Ramanujan graph of girth at least ten,
let \(K_d\) be its normalized biadjacency operator, and put

$$
P_d:=K_dK_d^*
\quad\text{on }L^2(L_d,\mathrm{unif}).
\tag{DR88.6}
$$

Then \(P_d\) is self-adjoint, Markov, and positive semidefinite as an
\(L^2\) operator; the assertion is not strict positivity of every kernel
entry. The Ramanujan bound gives

$$
\|P_dQ_d\|
\leq
\frac{4(d-1)}{d^2}
\longrightarrow0.
\tag{DR88.7}
$$

A two-step \(P_d\)-path has a natural lift obtained by sampling its hidden
\(R_d,L_d,R_d\) vertices. This lift is a four-edge walk in \(G_d\). With
probability

$$
q_d=(1-1/d)^3\longrightarrow1,
\tag{DR88.8}
$$

it has no immediate reversal. Girth at least ten then makes it the unique
length-four path between its endpoints, so on this event those endpoints
determine the middle vertex. If \(N_d=|L_d|\), averaging the bridge Gramian
trace gives the
optimal-floor bound

$$
\boxed{
\kappa_d^{\mathrm{br}}
\leq
\frac{N_d}{N_d-1}
\left[1-(1-1/d)^3\right]
=
O(d^{-1}).}
\tag{DR88.9}
$$

Nevertheless \(\ker B_d^{\mathrm{br}}=\mathbb C1\): conditioning on equal
endpoints connects every positive-transition neighbor, and connectedness
propagates equality. Choose a subsequence with
\(\sup_i\|P_iQ_i\|<1\), local floors
\(\kappa_i^{\mathrm{br}}>0\) tending to zero, and only constants fixed by
each local bridge augmentation. Under simultaneous product evolution,
finite-coordinate ANOVA followed by martingale convergence yields one
reversible carrier with a positive-semidefinite Markov transfer,
\(\|PQ\|<1\), no nonconstant exactly recoverable middle function, and
bridge spectrum accumulating at zero.

This derived Ramanujan application proves a decisive separation:

$$
\boxed{
\text{transfer gap}
+
\text{no exact two-ended recovery}
\centernot\Longrightarrow
\text{bridge floor}.}
\tag{DR88.10}
$$

The missing predicate is quantitative exclusion of *approximate* two-ended
recovery. In general put

$$
D_-=I-(P^*)^nP^n,
\qquad
A=(\mathsf E_{0,2n}-\mathsf E_0)J_n,
\tag{DR88.11}
$$

nested projections give

$$
D_-=B_n^{\mathrm{br}}+A^*A.
\tag{DR88.12}
$$

For reversible \(P\), the first formula reduces to
\(D_-=I-P^{2n}\). Let \(Q\) be the reducing complement of the common fixed
space; then \(0\leq D_-\leq Q\). The extra endpoint's synergistic prediction
is \(A^*A\). Given a transfer floor \(D_-\geq\gamma Q\), the exact relative
