condition is

$$
\boxed{
D_-
\leq
C_{\mathrm{qf}}B_n^{\mathrm{br}},}
\tag{DR88.13}
$$

equivalently
\(A^*A\leq(C_{\mathrm{qf}}-1)B_n^{\mathrm{br}}\). It yields

$$
B_n^{\mathrm{br}}
\geq
\frac{\gamma}{C_{\mathrm{qf}}}Q.
\tag{DR88.14}
$$

For a Gaussian mode, \(C_{\mathrm{qf}}^{\rm opt}=1+e^{-2\omega\ell}\leq2\).
For Yang--Mills it must be proved.

There is a further continuum no-go. Let
\(\lambda_{3B}^{\mathrm{dis}}=(C_{\mathrm{BF}}^{\mathrm{dis}})^{-1}\)
be the full-carrier edge for the disjoint initial--interior--final cover.
Testing it on a normalized centered boundary-adjacent observable
\(F=f(X_1)\) gives

$$
\boxed{
\lambda_{3B}^{\mathrm{dis}}
\leq
\mathbb E\operatorname{Var}
\bigl(f(X_1)\mid X_0,X_{2n}\bigr)
\leq
1-\|Pf\|^2,}
\tag{DR88.15}
$$

and therefore
\[
\lambda_{3B}^{\mathrm{dis}}\leq1-\|PQ\|^2.
\]
If \(H_a\geq0\) is self-adjoint,
\(P_a=e^{-a_{\tau,a}H_a/(\hbar c)}\), and some normalized centered
\(f_a\in\mathcal D(H_a^{1/2})\) obeys
\(\|H_a^{1/2}f_a\|^2\leq E_*<\infty\), then

$$
\boxed{
C_{\mathrm{BF}}^{\mathrm{dis}}(a)
\geq
\frac{\hbar c}{2a_{\tau,a}E_*}.}
\tag{DR88.16}
$$

Thus the disjoint full-slab factorization constant must diverge in any
ordinary continuum limit retaining a finite-energy nonvacuum direction.
This does not assume a positive gap. Under the full-vertex-gauging and
no-extra-global-constraint hypotheses of the linked pure kinetic \(SU(2)\)
Wilson calculation, the shortest-loop eigenvalue gives the action-level
witness

$$
\boxed{
C_{\mathrm{BF}}^{\mathrm{dis}}
\geq
\frac{1}{
1-\left(I_2(x)/I_1(x)\right)^{2g(\Gamma)}
}
=
\frac{x}{3g(\Gamma)}+O(1),}
\tag{DR88.17}
$$

as \(x\to\infty\) with \(g(\Gamma)\) fixed. In the standard tree-level
anisotropic \(SU(2)\) Wilson normalization,
\(x=4a_s/(g_0^2a_\tau)\). The divergence is independent of the outer
half-slab depth. The failed object is the full disjoint block carrier, not
the fixed-physical midpoint bridge.

The repair keeps the open interior
\(A_0=\{1,\ldots,2n-1\}\) but thickens the boundary blocks to

$$
A_-=\{0,\ldots,r\},
\qquad
A_+=\{2n-r,\ldots,2n\},
\qquad
1\leq r<n.
\tag{DR88.18}
$$

These blocks overlap \(A_0\), have unit-weight coverage one, and omit the
midpoint. If \(\mathscr L_{3B}^{\mathrm{coll}}\) is their conditional
resampling form, then the exact midpoint compression remains

$$
\boxed{
J_n^*\mathscr L_{3B}^{\mathrm{coll}}J_n
=
B_n^{\mathrm{br}}.}
\tag{DR88.19}
$$

Consequently a collared block-factorization constant \(C_*\) gives
\(B_n^{\mathrm{br}}\geq C_*^{-1}Q\). The new Wilson target is to prove this
for the inner-slab marginal on cells \(0,\ldots,2n\), induced by a finite
cylinder after integrating the outer preparation cells into boundary
weights, with

$$
n(a)a_{\tau,a}\longrightarrow\ell_*>0,
\qquad
r(a)a_{\tau,a}\longrightarrow\delta\in(0,\ell_*),
\tag{DR88.20}
$$

and one \(C_*<\infty\) uniform in regulator, spatial volume, outer boundary
data, preparation depth, and retained gauge-flux sector; then pass to the
stationary Perron bridge while retaining diagonal boundary-charge and
vacuum-balance sectors. Before applying the block theorem one must declare
the Wilson time-cell carrier: either use the exact transfer-kernel chain of
full slice configurations, or augment the raw-link factor graph so that every
link variable belongs to exactly one cell and every plaquette incidence is
recorded. The midpoint state must be measurable from a declared midpoint
cell omitted by both collars; this makes both collar terms vanish when the
raw block form is compressed to a midpoint observable. An explicit slice
pushforward is required. If the raw exterior
sigma-algebra is finer than the two-endpoint algebra, conditioning
monotonicity gives a raw midpoint defect bounded above by
\(B_n^{\mathrm{br}}\), which is still sufficient when the raw defect has a
uniform lower frame. Equality with the bridge defect additionally requires a
proved Markov-shielding identity. Equivalence of the pushforward law with the
inner Wilson slab, including its boundary weights, is part of the
construction rather than notation. To claim a gauge-sector block generator,
the sector projection must commute with every block expectation; otherwise
the factorization claim should be restricted to the complete physical
midpoint-pullback class. Caputo--Parisi's
weighted theorem genuinely allows
overlapping blocks, but it does not directly cover Wilson plaquette
interactions or gauge quotients. A gauge-covariant factor-graph extension
plus a scale-adapted finite-size or RG criterion is the precise outstanding
project. Fixed microscopic strong-mixing constants and the now-refuted
disjoint full-carrier constant are not acceptable substitutes.

This is the strict algebraic version of "whole before part." The global
path law is not itself called unitary or nonunitary. Its conditional
expectations are noninvertible arrows. Their whole-law factorization forces
a local bridge distinction; the already proved transfer solder turns that
distinction into Euclidean attenuation; and only OS/Poincare reconstruction
supplies the later local unitary clock and invariant mass interpretation.

The history dilation makes the omitted component quantitative. If
\(e_0=J_0J_0^*\), then

$$
\mathcal R_n^{\mathrm{past}}
=(I-e_0)\mathscr U_{\rm hist}^nJ_0,
\qquad
\boxed{
(\mathcal R_n^{\mathrm{past}})^*\mathcal R_n^{\mathrm{past}}
=I-(P^*)^nP^n
=I-P^{2n}\quad\text{reversibly}.}
\tag{DR88.21}
$$

This is a Pythagorean prediction residue, not stochastic ontology. Its fixed-
physical-depth lower frame is equivalent to transfer attenuation on the
declared nonfixed carrier. The bridge residue is bounded above by this full
one-boundary defect because conditioning on both endpoints can only improve
prediction; its independent lower frame remains the noncircular target.

There is a complementary exact dilation square. On the stationary two-sided
path carrier, the invertible history shift has a unitary Koopman
implementation \(\mathscr U_{\rm hist}\), while the one-slice Euclidean
transfer is its compression
\(P^n=J_0^*\mathscr U_{\rm hist}^nJ_0\). The Markov law makes these
compressions a semigroup, and the mass-gap datum is a strict centered
compression angle at fixed physical thickness, not a gap in the unitary
history shift. The Lorentzian group \(e^{-itH/\hbar}\) obtained after full OS
reconstruction is a second unitary with a different native carrier; a Markov
OS realization may identify that carrier with the slice \(L^2\) space, but
not canonically with the whole-history shift. This makes the Copernican claim
maximally precise: whole relation, presentation map, Euclidean attenuation,
and clock automorphism are four typed objects, not four names for one process.

## DR89. The plaquette typing closes and the surface-response problem opens

[[collared-quasi-factorization-and-surface-response/inq|The collared
quasi-factorization audit]] gives the three-block route a sharper reduction.
Work first on a finite open Wilson cylinder

$$
\Lambda_{p,n}
=
\{-p,\ldots,2n+p\}\times\Sigma
\tag{DR89.1}
$$

with ordinary fixed exterior data. For \(1\leq r<n\), set

$$
\begin{aligned}
A_-&=\{-p,\ldots,r\}\times\Sigma,\\
A_0&=\{1,\ldots,2n-1\}\times\Sigma,\\
A_+&=\{2n-r,\ldots,2n+p\}\times\Sigma,\\
D&:=A_-\cup A_+.
\end{aligned}
\tag{DR89.2}
$$

The two blocks \(D\) and \(A_0\) cover the whole cylinder and overlap in
collars, while \(D\) omits the midpoint. Suppose the whole law obeys

$$
\operatorname{Ent}_\mu(g)
\leq
C_2\,\mu\!\left[
\operatorname{Ent}_D(g)+\operatorname{Ent}_{A_0}(g)
\right].
\tag{DR89.3}
$$

Linearization at the constant density gives the corresponding variance
inequality. For \(F=f(X_n)\), the \(D\)-term vanishes because resampling the
end collars cannot alter the midpoint observable. The complement of \(A_0\)
contains both marked endpoints and may also contain outer preparation
variables, so conditioning monotonicity gives

$$
\mu[\operatorname{Var}_{A_0}(f(X_n))]
\leq
\mathbb E\!\left[
\operatorname{Var}(f(X_n)\mid X_0,X_{2n})
\right].
\tag{DR89.4}
$$

Therefore

$$
\boxed{
B_n^{\mathrm{br}}\geq C_2^{-1}Q.}
\tag{DR89.5}
$$

This is stronger as a proof strategy than the earlier exact-compression
requirement. One may retain the larger fixed-boundary cylinder, prove a lower
bound on its more strongly conditioned midpoint defect, and only afterward
pass the outer preparation depth to the stationary Perron law. Exact equality
of raw and two-endpoint conditional expectations is unnecessary. A literal
three-block entropy inequality follows by conditionally tensorizing the two
separated components of \(D\) on an open cylinder, or by two sequential
two-block applications, but neither step is needed for (DR89.5).

Cesi's 2001 theorem now closes the apparent hyperinteraction gap at finite
regulator. It treats finite-range summable Gibbs interactions under
Dobrushin--Shlosman complete analyticity and imposes no topology on the
single-spin space. Put all positively oriented links leaving a site into one
cell spin,

$$
\sigma_x=(U_{x,1},\ldots,U_{x,4})\in SU(N)^4.
\tag{DR89.6}
$$

The plaquette

$$
U_{x,\mu}U_{x+\hat\mu,\nu}
U_{x+\hat\nu,\mu}^{-1}U_{x,\nu}^{-1}
\tag{DR89.7}
$$

depends only on

$$
\{x,x+\hat\mu,x+\hat\nu\}.
\tag{DR89.8}
$$

Its support has diameter one in the lattice \(\ell^\infty\) metric. Relative
to the product prior \(\operatorname{Haar}_{SU(N)}^{\otimes4}\) at every
cell, the Wilson action is thus a bounded range-one three-cell interaction.
Gauge redundancy is an invariance of the full-support law, not a hard
constraint on its support. Cesi's interaction class applies to this raw
cell carrier; the nearest-neighbor pair restriction in Caputo--Parisi is no
longer the relevant obstruction.

The obstruction appears in Cesi's quantitative sufficient condition. If
\(\alpha_{\mathrm{CA}}\) is the complete-analyticity decay rate,
\(\ell\) is the distance between the two nonoverlapping complements, and
\(b\) is the number of boundary cells in the interaction collar, the proof
controls the dependence defect by

$$
\varepsilon
\leq
e\,bK e^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{CA}}\ell}.
\tag{DR89.9}
$$

For (DR89.2), the Wilson interaction range is one and

$$
\ell=r+1,
\qquad
b=2|\Sigma|.
\tag{DR89.10}
$$

Under the complete-analyticity hypotheses, at a fixed regulator this gives a
valid finite-volume theorem when
\[
r\gtrsim
\alpha_{\mathrm{CA}}^{-1}\bigl(\log|\Sigma|+O(1)\bigr).
\]
It is not the continuum estimate sought here. At fixed physical transverse
size in four dimensions, \(|\Sigma|\asymp a^{-3}\). In the favorable regime
where \(K\) has no compensating \(a^3\) suppression and the certificate rate
has finite physical scaling,
\(\alpha_{\mathrm{CA}}(a)r(a)=O(1)\); for example one may test
\(\alpha_{\mathrm{CA}}(a)\asymp a/\xi_{\mathrm{phys}}\) with a fixed
physical collar \(r(a)\asymp\delta/a\). The exponential in (DR89.9) is then
only of order \(e^{-\delta/\xi_{\mathrm{phys}}}\), whereas the displayed
surface factor grows like \(a^{-3}\). Thus the sufficient estimate becomes
vacuous under that scaling. Complete-analyticity decay is stronger than an
ordinary physical correlation-length statement, so this scaling must itself
be proved. At fixed regulator the same certificate loses volume uniformity
unless another constant compensates the growth of \(|\Sigma|\).

This diagnoses a proof loss, not a physical no-go. Product bridges give the
counter-principle: their maximal correlation is the maximum of the component
correlations, so their bridge floor does not pay once per transverse cell.
An \(L^\infty\) telescoping proof can therefore lose surface-uniformity that
the underlying Hilbert geometry retains.

That geometry has an exact operator form. Let

$$
P_D=\mathbb E[\cdot\mid\mathcal F_{D^c}],
\qquad
P_0=\mathbb E[\cdot\mid\mathcal F_{A_0^c}],
\tag{DR89.11}
$$

and let \(R\) project onto their common range. Before sector restriction, the
finite Wilson density is strictly positive relative to product Haar and the
cover is complete, so the common coordinate sigma-algebra is trivial. A
sectorized claim requires the sector projection to reduce both conditional
expectations and a proof identifying their meet there; arbitrary exterior
data can break the symmetry that defined the sector.
The reduced Friedrichs cosine is

$$
c_F(D,A_0)
:=
\|(P_D-R)(P_0-R)\|.
\tag{DR89.12}
$$

The two-projection theorem gives

$$
\boxed{
(I-P_D)+(I-P_0)
\geq
(1-c_F(D,A_0))(I-R).}
\tag{DR89.13}
$$

For a centered midpoint observable \(F\), \(P_DF=F\), and the adjoint
orientation of (DR89.12) gives
\(\|(P_0-R)F\|\leq c_F(D,A_0)\|F\|\). Direct compression therefore yields
the sharper bridge estimate

$$
\boxed{
B_n^{\mathrm{br}}
\geq
(1-c_F(D,A_0)^2)Q.}
\tag{DR89.14}
$$

For independent transverse components the reduced maximal correlation
tensorizes by a maximum rather than a sum. The next Wilson theorem can
therefore be stated without poetry:

$$
\boxed{
\sup_{a,L,p,\tau,\mathfrak s}
c_F^{(a,L,p,\tau,\mathfrak s)}(D,A_0)
\leq
\rho_*<1,}
\tag{DR89.15}
$$

with \(n(a)a_\tau\to\ell_*>0\) and
\(r(a)a_\tau\to\delta\in(0,\ell_*)\), uniformly in spatial volume \(L\),
preparation depth \(p\), exterior data \(\tau\), regulator \(a\), and every
retained neutral sector \(\mathfrak s\) proved to reduce both block
expectations. One may instead state the norm on an explicitly declared
restricted midpoint subspace.

Equation (DR89.15) is an exact stopping condition, not a derivation. In
Markov examples, closely related projection angles are the transfer
contraction itself, so postulating the angle would be circular. The
noncircular task is to construct a complete \(L^2\) boundary-response
analysis from Wilson plaquette incidence, covariant derivatives, Hessian or
Fisher response, and finite-range geometry; prove that it dominates the
reduced projection product; and bound its norm by \(\rho_*\) without using
the unknown transfer spectrum. A linear Hessian bound alone is not enough
unless a comparison theorem extends it to the complete nonlinear
\(L^2\) distinction carrier.

The literature boundary is now clean. Shen--Zhu--Zhu prove volume-uniform
gradient log-Sobolev and Poincare inequalities for continuous \(SU(N)\)
Wilson theory in an explicit strong-coupling regime, but on periodic tori
and for the Langevin form rather than (DR89.3) under every frozen exterior
configuration. Stroock--Zegarlinski give the abstract equivalence between
uniform log-Sobolev control and Dobrushin--Shlosman mixing for compact
continuous spins. Chatterjee identifies arbitrary-boundary exponential
decay as a sufficient gauge-theoretic route to center symmetry and
confinement, but assumes that decay. Adhikari--Cao prove a weak-coupling
decay theorem for finite gauge groups, not continuous \(SU(N)\), arbitrary
boundary entropy factorization, or the four-dimensional continuum limit.
No located theorem supplies all of (DR89.15).

This also gives the strict algebraic reading of the latest Copernican clue.
The whole probability law is an object, not an evolution operator, so
unitary/nonunitary is not yet a well-typed alternative. Its conditional
expectations are faithful as positive maps for the faithful finite Wilson
law, but noninjective and noninvertible as presentation arrows. Their relative
position can force a positive residue on every nonconstant local distinction.
Reflection-positive OS/Poincare reconstruction independently supplies a
reversible unitary clock on the selected physical carrier; identifying the
bridge with its Euclidean transfer then converts the residue floor into a
lower bound on the clock generator. If “ontological time” names anything
exact at this stage, it names the directed order of composition and forgotten
fibres, not the one-parameter clock group. The construction does not say that
forgetting manufactures unitarity; it says that an information-losing
whole-to-part presentation and a separately reconstructed local unitary
grammar can fit in one covariant diagram.

The progress ledger is therefore:

1. the midpoint bridge reduction is now two-block rather than three-block;
2. the Wilson plaquette fits the established finite-range interaction class
   without a new factor-graph theorem;
3. the known proof fails at a precisely located surface-cardinality factor;
4. a tensorizing \(L^2\) surface-response angle is the new carrier-level
   theorem; and
5. its uniform action-derived bound, continuum passage, OS reconstruction,
   unique vacuum, locality, and Poincare mass interpretation remain open.

## Kill conditions

- A transfer gap and the qualitative identity
  \(\ker B^{\rm br}=\mathbb C1\) are said to imply a bridge floor; positive
  reversible high-girth expanders have both while their bridge floors tend to
  zero through approximate two-ended recovery.
- The Gaussian identity
  \(\kappa=\tanh(\omega\ell)\) is applied to an interacting bridge, or the
  bridge Gramian is identified with \(\tanh(\ell H/(\hbar c))\) on every
  chaos. Only the sharp Gaussian bottom edge obeys the stated inverse.
- Positivity of a finite linear conditional-covariance Schur complement is
  used as a lower bound on the full bridge residue outside a Gaussian or
  endpoint-complete predictor class; nonlinear endpoint functions can reduce
  the true residual further.
- A one-slice sampler factorization is substituted for the three-block
  factorization of the actual spacetime law, or whole-slab strong mixing is
  inferred from the desired transfer gap or exponential clustering.
- The Caputo--Parisi nearest-neighbor pair theorem is applied directly to raw
  Wilson link hyperedges. Cesi's finite-range theorem repairs the interaction
  typing after the cell-spin reorganization, but does not remove its
  complete-analyticity hypothesis.
- Cesi's finite-volume quasi-factorization is called continuum-uniform while
  its proof-level dependence defect still carries the diverging transverse
  boundary cardinality, or an \(L^2\) surface angle is merely assumed instead
  of derived from the Wilson action.
- The proper inclusion of a composite GNS correspondence in a fused
  correspondence is called a transfer gap; its canonical endpoint lift has
  zero fusion residue. The middle-insertion bridge analysis supplies a
  nonzero history-sensitive solder, but only a regulator-uniform lower frame
  for its Gramian can imply a gap.
- A globally normalized path tilt is assigned the centered conditional bridge
  score, or the GNS half-density tangent is given the full Fisher norm instead
  of one quarter of it.
- Fiberwise Doob/Perron cancellation is said to remove the vacuum vector from
  the global bridge norm; the endpoint base law remains Perron weighted.
- The bridge-variance lower frame is called equivalent to a transfer gap, or
  full support at one finite regulator is called a uniform lower frame. The
  implication is one-way without further hypotheses, and its constant must
  survive fixed-physical-slab continuum scaling.
- Pointwise GNS fibers of a CP semigroup are called a product system although
  their multiplication is only coisometric; a product-system envelope is a
  further completion or dilation.
- The one-time algebras $\sigma(X_k)$ are called a reverse filtration, or the
  future predictor is written with $P$ rather than $P^*$ without reversibility.
- The balanced periodic two-cut Hilbert--Schmidt law is identified with the
  fixed-inner-slab stationary Doob law obtained by sending only the outer
  preparation depth to infinity.
- A reversible Markov operator is assumed Hilbert-positive; a period-two
  $-1$ mode has zero $I-P^2$ innovation defect and blocks a Hamiltonian
  logarithm.
- A first-order entropy or area differential is identified with the even projective Hamiltonian Rayleigh form without a ray-to-path construction or a second-order same-tangent response map.
- A discrete Fuchsian group is said to force discrete spectrum without checking its cusp-invariant subspace; or the toric fiber filling is treated as a boundary condition for the hyperbolic base Laplacian.
- The integer unit defect $p=\pm1$ is identified with spectral coercivity: the automorphic operator sees only $p\bmod12$, and every nonzero residue removes the cusp zero mode.
- The nonunitary unipotent $GL_4(\mathbb Z)$ monodromy is used as though it were the unitary twist of a positive self-adjoint automorphic Laplacian.
- A triangle-group character is relabelled as $SU(3)$ adjoint holonomy without checking cusp invariants; every compact-group adjoint holonomy fixes the Lie algebra of a maximal torus.
- Riemannian $\mathfrak H\cong PSL_2(\mathbb R)/PSO(2)$ is identified with Lorentzian $AdS_2\cong PSL_2(\mathbb R)/SO_0(1,1)$ because they share an ambient Lie group; or an integrable complex structure on spacetime is declared a premise of ordinary local QFT.
- The static vacuum density, its entropy, its Hessian, or the Einstein ledger-to-area compliance is claimed to fix a transfer rate; the rescaled-generator family \(\varepsilon L\) leaves all of those one-slice data unchanged.
- A fusion step, categorical iteration, or lazified GNS contraction is called physical time before an injective transfer realization and independently calibrated duration are constructed.
- Boundedness of a carrier map is claimed to bound its countable matrix of block norms; direct sums of normalized Walsh matrices give an exact counterexample.
- Every stage of a changing-carrier chain is required to be strictly contractive, thereby missing complementary norm-one stages whose ordered product contracts all nonvacuum directions; or individual spectral radii are substituted for the norm of the actual ordered product.
- A real Connes cocycle is said to change the natural-cone vacuum, or an imaginary half-density amplitude is called unitary; under genuine state change the frame unitary and pointing amplitude are different maps, and the target vacuum alignment must generally be recomputed.
- A subunit product of lossy scale maps is called an energy gap without an independent intertwiner to the injective reflection-positive slab transfer on one reconstructed carrier.
- A raw unbounded Hessian square root, gradient, or Dirac factor is placed beneath the bounded transfer defect; any such dominated analysis must extend to a bounded contraction, so a heat or resolvent transform and its normalization are mandatory.
- The order inequality $I-A\geq\eta R$ is squared even though $A$ and $R$ need not commute; the safe positive-transfer implication is $I-A^2\geq I-A\geq\eta R$.
- A trivial transported response kernel, or a floor reached only at infinite horizon, is substituted for a regulator-uniform lower frame across one finite physical slab.
- A noninjective expectation is called the finite-thickness physical transfer rather than an auxiliary response analysis, or a joint-correspondence compression is called physical before its endpoint map is intertwined with the Yang--Mills transfer.
- A one-step defect column or changing-carrier cascade is called a Sz.-Nagy dilation without one fixed enlarged endomorphism and the compression-of-powers identity.
- The direct sum of all chronological stage-defect spaces is called the minimal completion of the endpoint product; only its reachable residue range is isometrically equivalent to the endpoint defect carrier, and the full archive may contain unreachable coordinates.
- A proper isometry is called ontologically irreversible merely because it is not onto, although its adjoint recovers every compatible history on its range.
- Wold purity, strong pointwise decay, or complete infinite-horizon residue capture is called a mass gap without a finite-depth uniform operator-norm or lower-frame estimate.
- A direct sum of Hilbert defect coordinates is called a product system, an $E_0$-semigroup, a durable record algebra, or an outcome instrument without the corresponding multiplication, endomorphisms, inclusions, state, and readout map.
- The canonical defect space is treated as a physically selected record carrier merely because it is universal among isometric norm completions.
- The quantum-dimension offset in normalized Wilson multiplication is called a mass gap even though it is a scalar energy translation that cancels from normalized Gibbs states and excitation differences.
- The formal distributional \(1\)-eigenstate or flat-connection evaluation functional of Wilson multiplication is treated as a normalizable Yang--Mills vacuum, or a canonical subfactor hypergroup action is called vacuum ergodic although it fixes the whole smaller-algebra GNS subspace.
- The GNS disagreement \(I-V_\Phi\) is identified with BKM forgetting; the reversible binary flip has maximal disagreement and zero information loss.
- A finite-dimensional response Hessian, or any finite collection of such readouts, is said to cover an infinite-dimensional physical vacuum complement without a lower-frame theorem.
- A regional entropy Hessian is claimed to dominate a vacuum-only categorical defect without proving that centralizer and commutant-invisible directions lie in the categorical kernel and that the joint regional analysis map has a regulator-uniform relative lower frame; Reeh--Schlieder density is not such a theorem.
- Strict descent, a nonfaithful realization, and local unitary clock evolution are treated as one map; a faithful expectation is claimed to create an exact automorphic sector despite faithful-descent rigidity; or a Stinespring representation is promoted from a factorization to a reversible hidden ontology.
- A unit of the retained corner \(e\operatorname{End}(W)e\) is called a whole-carrier unitary by silently replacing the corner identity \(e\) with \(I_W\).
- An arbitrary positive short or Schur complement is assumed to be a Markov, completely Dirichlet, or Lindblad generator without proving the relevant order and matrix-amplification properties.
- Exact invisibility of the whole zero modes is treated as sufficient for a local gap; the uniform lift inequality is what excludes almost-zero whole modes with normalized nonvacuum local image.
- Marginalization is said to create stiffness, despite the exact subtraction of conditional score covariance, or a globally positive smooth potential Hessian is assumed on the closed compact carrier \(SU(3)^E\).
- Distinction maps are defined from the Hamiltonian spectrum or fitted low-energy correlators.
- Response-fiber norms or wall measures are freely rescaled after the desired coefficient is known.
- Descent, a torsor, a conditional expectation, an instrument branch, and an actual character are treated as one operation.
- A deterministic factor presentation of a stochastic law is promoted from an exact representation theorem to proof of deterministic ontology, or a noninjective readout is claimed to generate probabilities without a state.
- Directional distinction operators are multiplied before they have been transported to one common carrier and proved to strongly commute.
- Several HSMI generators are combined before their compatibility, strong commutation, and common Poincare representation have been proved.
- A normalizable pointing is treated as sufficient for coercivity, despite normalizable heavy-tailed counterexamples.
- The homogeneous-partner law, a binary generator normalization, or the incoming-density boundary law is described as derived from core normality alone.
- The translation-Haar relative-density carrier is treated as forced by core normality; on the natural core-trace carrier the same factor is unitarily equivalent to \(A_\nu\) and has no index jump.
- The critical non-Fredholm operator \(B_{1/2}\) is assigned an index, or its Fredholm phase integer is identified with a bosonic excitation gap.
- The exact OS interface unitary \(B_\rho^{\mathrm{OS}}\) is treated as the entire physical-to-scale map while the interface-to-slice and boundary-to-log-scale maps are omitted.
- Projection-coded log odds and the normalized-involution parameter are called the same canonical coordinate; this silently changes \(\nu\) by a factor of two.
- The scale-shadow edge \(\nu^2\) is called physical mass before a physical-to-wall analysis map and same-carrier Casimir solder are constructed.
- The resolvent-logistic profile constants \(1/4\), \(1/12\), two nats, or \(\pi^2/3\) are called a mass scale even though the same constants occur for gapless input operators.
- Spectral channels are recentered to one logistic profile and their centers are then discarded; this removes exactly the information that distinguishes a lower edge from accumulation at zero.
- Strong saturation \(Z_N\to I-E_L(\{0\})\), which holds for every nonnegative self-adjoint \(L\), is confused with norm-uniform saturation on that support carrier, which is equivalent to a positive lower edge; the kernel projection is not \(P_0\) until a kernel--vacuum theorem identifies it.
- Products of two conditional-expectation shell projections are called joint incidence projections without a commuting-square or strong-commutation theorem.
- The intersection of two terminal tails is the vacuum, but either tail contains additional nonvacuum vectors; those one-sided sectors remain in the kernel of the paired product.
- The automatic smallest weight of a finite shell tower is called a mass gap without a regulator-uniform bound through the unbounded-depth limit.
- A response operator is set to \(e^{2pA}\), or shell weights are fitted to cancel \(e^{-2pa}\), and the resulting identity is presented as a derived wall.
- The Pimsner--Popa order inequality \(E(x)\geq\operatorname{Ind}(E)^{-1}x\) is retyped as a lower Hilbert-space frame bound for the \(L^2\) implementation of \(E\), which is projection-like and has a kernel for a proper inclusion.
- The normalized identity \(V_E^*V_E=1\), the discrete spectrum of allowed subfactor indices, or the jump from categorical dimension one to \(d>1\) is called a Hamiltonian or glueball gap.
- The additive relative-entropy capacity \(2n\log d\) is silently replaced by the multiplicative horizon law \(\iota_n/\iota_b=d^{2n}\), or the latter is described as a consequence of index theory rather than an independent effective-cell-count weld.
- A Fredholm index, Jones--Kosaki index, descent-loss Hessian, and Poincare-Casimir lower edge are treated as one invariant without explicit carrier maps.
- A relaxation gap \(\gamma\) from \(T_t=E+e^{-\gamma t}(I-E)\) is advertised as derived from descent even though \(\gamma>0\) was freely chosen.
- Boundary area, entropy extensivity, or the number of readout channels is treated as a lower-frame response bound; shared blind directions are not excluded.
- The balance \(D-2=2p\) is advertised as an independent selection of four dimensions after the Yang--Mills action's known engineering dimensions were used to choose either exponent.
- A one-sided spatial RG address operator is called mass even though unbounded scale covariance forces it gapless and no Lorentz or Casimir solder has been constructed.
- One oriented null-pair product is identified pointwise with the \(3+1\)-dimensional Casimir, or its floor is called a mass gap without proving that it is the physical null-translation pair on the full vacuum complement of a positive-energy Lorentz-covariant representation. Under those hypotheses one fixed-direction floor does suffice; a common floor on a countable dense direction set is needed only for direct pointwise Casimir tomography.
- The interface operator used in the resolvent transform is chosen from the Hamiltonian, Casimir, glueball eigenvectors, or fitted mass and then presented as an independent explanation.
- Scalar rescaling covariance of the resolvent family is called QFT naturality without regional intertwining or quantitative quasilocal tail control.
- Wilson flow is called a noninjective collapse at finite regulator, despite being an invertible field-space diffeomorphism there, or its log-time and log-length coordinates are conflated to hide a factor of two.
- Type-\(\mathrm{III}_1\) structure is claimed to select a massive representation or a Casimir floor.
- A Poincare Casimir, an internal gauge-group Casimir, a measurement-context character, and the center of a represented observable algebra are treated as the same “central” object.
- A selected measurement outcome or broken-symmetry phase is asserted to imply a positive Hamiltonian lower bound.
- A unique vacuum point, irreducible fixed-mass sector, or normalizable pointing is asserted to imply a gap without excluding spectrum accumulating at zero.
- Adjoining a complex structure, braid phase, or unitary polar factor is claimed to open a positive gap even though it leaves the relevant singular values or Friedrichs angle unchanged.
- Touching past and future halves are used for the angle theorem; their common time-zero carrier removes the transfer datum. Or a raw adjacent-slice angle floor is required to remain nonzero as the lattice spacing vanishes.
- The Jordan eigenvalue-discriminant wall is identified with determinant/rank loss, black-hole entropy, or a mass gap; these are different loci and physical claims.
- A WKB coordinate envelope is called a Born dwell law before the symmetric Hilbert measure, self-adjoint domain, and current are specified.
- The wall family has a common kernel larger than the vacuum or only pointwise, non-uniform separation.
- The energy-solder inequality is simply postulated after inspecting the desired gap.
- The dimensional scale imports the observed gap, measured $G$, Planck units, or a fitted cosmic history while claiming a derivation.
- Newton's constant is made essential to the pure Yang--Mills gap without a \(G\to0\) theorem at fixed renormalized Yang--Mills scale that recovers the observable net, state, translations, and energy form and carries a gap estimate uniform in the decoupling parameter.
- A metrological unit change is identified with a Weyl rescaling, or a Poincare spectral mass is treated as a spacetime scale field without a comparison map.
- Horizon area saturation, relative-entropy positivity, or a thermal/KMS state is claimed to imply a positive Hamiltonian gap; massless Rindler QFT is an immediate counterexample.
- A black hole is declared to have no interior while exterior charges, response, ringdown, horizon thermodynamics, and information accounting are left unreconstructed.
- A Markov or modular generator is called physical time without OS reconstruction or a direct positive-energy intertwiner.
- Bare BKM or relative-entropy geometry is claimed to determine a dimensional rate despite the fixed-state rescaling family \(\mathcal L_\varepsilon=\varepsilon(E_\sigma-\mathrm{id})\).
- One modular logarithm or HSMI translation is called mass instead of reconstructing the full translation representation and testing its joint Poincare Casimir.
- Causal boundedness is treated as a UV cutoff, a finite-dimensional algebra, or a finite information-capacity theorem.
- A single totally ordered patch chain is treated as sufficient to reconstruct spacelike locality.
- The transverse defect that forms the observable carrier is reused as its internal gap form even though it vanishes on that carrier.
- A finite-spacing Wilson transfer Hamiltonian is silently identified with the canonical Kogut--Susskind Hamiltonian, or its logarithm is claimed to dominate the unbounded group Laplacian on the full carrier despite the high-character \(SU(2)\) counterexample.
- The injective finite-depth Euclidean semigroup \(e^{-\ell H/(\hbar c)}\) is called literal forgetting or wavefunction collapse; its gap controls uniform attenuation, while loss requires a separate quotient, expectation, restriction, or instrument.
- Locality of the bulk Euclidean action is used to assume locality or quasilocality of the integrated boundary action.
- Coercivity holds only in one topological sector, one observable channel, fixed volume, or fixed cutoff.
- Vacuum projections, locality, covariance, gauge identities, or nontrivial observables fail in the continuum limit.
- Low-energy agreement is checked only for one state or finite observable list, without natural comparison maps and a uniform remainder.
